  - `links` list of object: keep only turns that touched these (a file path or name, a commit hash, a url), or sit next to one that did
    - `ref` string, required
    - `kind` one of "file", "read", "commit", "url"
  - `machines` "all" or "local"
  - `per_session` integer
  - `prefix` boolean: each word as a prefix, all of them, keyword only: for completion while typing
  - `project_cwds` list of string
  - `role` "user" or "assistant"
  - `sessions` list of string: also these sessions wherever they ran (a project's attached sessions); from modules and the person's surfaces only
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `recall.sessions`

Indexed sessions, newest first, optionally only those in or under a folder, since a time, started by a person, or with the given ids.

- Input:
  - `agent` string
  - `cwd` string
  - `human` boolean
  - `ids` list of string
  - `limit` integer
  - `machines` "all" or "local"
  - `since` number
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `recall.setup`

Install the search model now (the library and its weights, once) and load it, so search ranks by meaning. Resolves when it is ready or has failed, and says which.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `recall.status`

How much is indexed, when the last pass ran, and whether search can rank by meaning.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `recall.thread`

One session and its turns, in order. Takes a session id or an unambiguous prefix of one.

- Input:
  - `session` string, required
  - `agent` string
  - `from` integer
  - `limit` integer
  - `machines` "all" or "local"
  - `source` "box" or "mac"
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `recall.transcript`

A rich read of one session for a person's own screen: what was said, thinking, every tool call with its input and output, and each turn's time and tokens. Takes a session id or an unambiguous prefix of one. Without from, the last blocks; before pages back.

- Input:
  - `session` string, required
  - `before` integer
  - `from` integer
  - `limit` integer
  - `machines` "all" or "local"
  - `source` "box" or "mac"
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `recall.turn`

A span of one past session, word for word: the turns themselves, no summary, each with its pointer (session:seq), its time, and what it touched (files, commits, urls). Name the turn with seq, and before and after for the turns around it, or give from with to or span. A turn the search index had to cut is read whole from the transcript. Redacted like everything Recall holds.

- Input:
  - `session` string, required: a session id, or an unambiguous prefix of one
  - `after` integer
  - `agent` string
  - `before` integer
  - `from` integer
  - `full` boolean: false: give a long turn as the index holds it (cut) instead of reading the transcript
  - `seq` integer
  - `span` integer
  - `to` integer
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `recall.unwatch`

Stop following a session (a watch id from recall.watch).

- Input:
  - `watch` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `recall.watch`

Follow one session live: each completed turn arrives as a session.turn event (thread = the session id) and session.state says whether a reply is under way. from is a turn id to replay after first; without it, only new turns. Call again with the same watch id to renew it: a watch nobody renews ends after 3 minutes, and one whose session is quiet for 30 minutes ends too.

- Input:
  - `session` string, required
  - `from` string
  - `watch` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

## records

### `records.actors`

The people of a Space: who each is and the role they hold. Only what the caller's role may see.

- Input:
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.create`

Make a record of a type.

- Input:
  - `data` object, required
  - `type` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.define`

Add or change record types and their fields (a DefineDiff). The kernel decides who may.

- Input:
  - `diff` object, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.dev-seed`

DEVELOPMENT ONLY, while the presence stand-in is on: seed a Space with the walk's types, records and tasks. Otherwise it does nothing.

- Input:
  - `space` string
- Callers: `cli`, `local`

### `records.events`

The Space's event log as the caller may read it: for a record, for a task, or all; newest last.

- Input:
  - `limit` integer
  - `record` string
  - `since` integer
  - `space` string
  - `task` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.forget`

Forget one record for good: the text of every task about it is emptied, the store destroys it and its history, its events lose their data, and its sealed values are destroyed. Linked files stay in Files. Cannot be undone. Customize-level (admin and owner), the person's own act with their presence; an assistant proposes it with records.forget.propose.

- Input:
  - `urn` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`
- Needs a person present.

### `records.forget.propose`

Ask the person to forget one record for good. Nothing is forgotten: a task goes to the person, who does it themselves with their presence. For an assistant, which may only propose.

- Input:
  - `urn` string, required
  - `why` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.get`

One record by its reference, or null when it is not there or not yours to see.

- Input:
  - `urn` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.holders`

Who holds one role: a page of role records, each naming its holder. `role` is a role type, `stage` narrows to one stage. Ended roles are left out unless `include_ended` is true. Only rows the caller may read.

- Input:
  - `role` string, required
  - `cursor` string
  - `include_ended` boolean
  - `limit` integer
  - `space` string
  - `stage` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.kits.get`

One Kit from the library in the form the Flows tools take (flows.kit.card to see what it would do, flows.kit.propose to ask for the install).

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.kits.library`

The Kits this build ships, before anything is installed: id, name, version, a plain description and what each adds (types, templates, roles, flows, views, sealed fields).

- Input:
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.linked`

Everything that links to a record (the reverse of a link field), across types, only what the caller may read. `truncated` is true when more exist than `limit` (default 50, at most 200).

- Input:
  - `urn` string, required
  - `field` string
  - `limit` integer
  - `type` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.list`

One page of records of a type: filter, sort, a cursor from the last page. include_deleted lists the removed ones too (the Bin): your own, or all for an owner or admin.

- Input:
  - `type` string, required
  - `cursor` string
  - `filter` any
  - `include_deleted` boolean
  - `limit` integer
  - `sort` list
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.me`

The person making this call, and the Space it acts in.

- Input:
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.reference`

A record put in front of the AI (the # in the composer): the record as data, with every sealed part (and any part this room may not read) a {{field:urn#name}} placeholder, never a value. Null when it is not there or not yours to see.

- Input:
  - `urn` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.reveal`

Show a sealed value to the person on their own screen, once. Human-only: the person's presence proof rides beside the request and the chain must be exactly one person.

- Input:
  - `field` string, required
  - `purpose` string, required
  - `urn` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.roles`

What a contact or organization is to the Space: every role record that points at it (prospect, client, ambassador, whatever the Space defined), current ones first. Ended roles are included unless `include_ended` is false. Only roles the caller may read.

- Input:
  - `urn` string, required
  - `include_ended` boolean
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.seal-put`

Put a value into a record's sealed field. It goes straight to the sealing process and never rides the record; the record keeps only the reference. The person's own act.

- Input:
  - `field` string, required
  - `urn` string, required
  - `value` string, required
  - `class` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.sees-as`

The record as the person sees it, or as their assistant would (sealed fields only as placeholders, and only the fields its grants allow).

- Input:
  - `urn` string, required
  - `who` "person" or "assistant", required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.types`

The record types of a Space, as defined (a type may carry kind: project). The kernel's own bookkeeping types are left out unless `system: true` is asked for, and then carry system: true.

- Input:
  - `space` string
  - `system` boolean
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.update`

Change a record's fields; a stale base_version is refused (version_conflict).

- Input:
  - `base_version` integer, required
  - `patch` object, required
  - `urn` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.workspace.create`

Internal, for the spaces module: confirm a new Space has its store. The built-in store is always there.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
  - `name` string
  - `store` string
- Callers: `module`

### `records.workspace.delete`

Internal, for the spaces module: a Space's store goes with the Space, never by this call.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: `module`

## relay

### `relay.apply`

Turn the relay on at a signed instruction from the owner's app. Only the wink module calls it, after verifying the signature.

- Input:
  - `url` string
- Callers: any caller

### `relay.code.alloc`

Ask the relay for a free typed-code rendezvous for this box (5 minutes, one at a time). Answers { rv, exp }, or null when the relay is busy, away or has no typed codes. Modules only.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.code.release`

Give the typed-code rendezvous back. Modules only.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.code.reply`

Answer a typed code's message (the relay.code-asked event): q is its id, m the reply in base64url, or none to refuse. Modules only.

- Input:
  - `q` string, required
  - `m` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.device.info`

A paired relay device as the link module's companion check needs it: kind, trusted, when it paired, its presence key id and whether it was removed. Null for an id never paired. Modules only.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.device.presence`

The presence key id enrolled for a paired relay device, or null.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.devices.admit-server`

Admit a paired server's relay key as a row of kind server (never an app device): it may open one peer stream, and the home's door lets it read the network's status. `pub` is the 32-byte key, base64url; `server` is its Wink device id. Modules only. Answers { id }.

- Input:
  - `pub` string, required
  - `server` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.devices.all`

The paired computers and phones, for a module: id, name, kind and whether each is connected now.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `relay.devices.ask-trust`

A browser paired from the hosted web app asks the owner to trust it fully. Only that browser, about itself; it tells every surface once (device.trust-asked) and the owner's own relay.devices.trust, with presence, is the approval.

- Input: none
- Callers: `web`

### `relay.devices.clear-leftover`

On a server nobody owns: let go of every paired device row left by a pairing that never completed ownership, so a new owner's pairing is not refused by them. Only the Wink module asks, and only while the server is unowned. Answers { cleared }.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `relay.devices.drop`

Close a paired device's connections and refuse it from now on, for a module that has just removed it for the owner.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `relay.devices.drop-server`

Take a paired server's relay row away and close its channels. Modules only. Answers { dropped }.

- Input:
  - `server` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.devices.list`

Devices paired through the relay: id, name, when paired and last seen, whether presence is enrolled, and whether it is connected now.

- Input: none
- Callers: `web`

### `relay.devices.node`

A paired relay device's own Noise identity and, if it has reported one, its tailnet node, for a module to check ownership or open a direct connection, never for a person or a device to call about itself.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.devices.path`

A paired device says which way it reaches the box now (relay or direct over the tailnet) and its measured round trip. Over the tailnet the first report carries the device id and the one-time code the relay path gave it, which links the device to its tailnet node.

- Input:
  - `path` "relay" or "direct", required
  - `code` string
  - `id` string
  - `rtt` number
- Callers: any caller

### `relay.devices.remove`

Remove a paired device: its connections close at once and it can no longer reach the box through the relay. Its presence key is removed too.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `relay.devices.rename`

Rename a paired device.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `name` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `relay.devices.trust`

Give a browser paired from the hosted web app the full powers of the owner's app (pairing devices, vault secrets), or take them back. Not callable from a web device that is not trusted.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `trusted` boolean, required
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `relay.disable`

Turn the relay off: the box stops connecting out, and every device connected through it is dropped. Paired devices stay paired.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `relay.enable`

Turn the relay on: the box connects out to the relay so paired devices can reach it from anywhere.

- Input:
  - `url` string
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `relay.join`

This Vyre becomes a device of another box, redeeming a one-time pairing code minted there (relay.pair.start or onboard.join{action:"relay"}). One redemption: the channel closes once paired, then this tool returns what the other box said (its name, this device's id, whether presence enrolled). becomeDevice, when true, flips this machine to "device" once paired (onboard.machine): the same flag onboard.join{action:"verify",becomeDevice} takes. Does not keep a connection open; that is not built yet. A pasted URL that is not a real Vyre pairing code is refused before any prompt. Not available on a Mac yet: see vyre-core (ADR 0040).

- Input:
  - `url` string, required
  - `becomeDevice` boolean
  - `name` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `relay.pair.first`

During onboarding only, before this box has any person on a device: make the QR code for the first device. Refused once a device is paired or a tailnet owner exists.

- Input: none
- Callers: `onboard`

### `relay.pair.pending.confirm`

The yes for a waiting pairing (a gated ticket, X-1): enrols the device that redeemed it, now. Only the Wink module, which has had the person pick the right three words. With `trusted` the device is marked trusted at the same moment (an owner-confirmed phone or computer). Answers { paired, id, key?, alg? }: the public key the device offered in its hello, so the pairing can bind its session to it.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `trusted` boolean
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.pair.start`

Make a QR code that pairs one more device with this box through the relay. The code works once, for 10 minutes; making a new one voids the last.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`
- Needs a person present.

### `relay.pair.ticket`

Mint a one-time pairing ticket for the Vyre code (Wink): a phone that scans it resolves the box's identity from the relay, then pairs exactly as relay.pair.start's QR does. Works once, for 5 minutes; call again for a fresh one (an old, unused ticket is simply left to expire, unlike relay.pair.start's single live QR). Not available on a Mac yet: see vyre-core (ADR 0040).

- Input:
  - `seed` string
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `relay.pair.window.close`

Close the pairing window: the live code is withdrawn at the relay.

- Input:
  - `window` string, required
- Callers: `deck`, `tailnet`

### `relay.pair.window.confirm`

The person confirms the phone that is pairing (shown as pairing.requested, with its name and key fingerprint). Only then is it enrolled, and the window closes. Answers { confirmed: true }.

- Input:
  - `device` string, required
  - `window` string, required
- Callers: `deck`, `tailnet`

### `relay.pair.window.open`

Open a pairing window on this screen: one proof, then up to 10 minutes of Wink codes this screen may renew without another prompt (relay.pair.window.renew). Answers { window, closesAt, pingEveryMs, ticket, ticketExpiresAt }. The window is for pair.device on this box only. A phone that redeems a code is enrolled only after relay.pair.window.confirm.

- Input: none
- Callers: `deck`, `tailnet`
- Needs a person present.

### `relay.pair.window.ping`

The open screen's heartbeat for its pairing window, every 15 seconds. The window closes after 30 seconds of silence. Answers { closesInMs }.

- Input:
  - `window` string, required
- Callers: `deck`, `tailnet`

### `relay.pair.window.reject`

"Not you?": refuse the phone that is waiting to be confirmed, shown as pairing.requested. The window stays open and the slot is free at once, so a stranger who redeemed a code cannot hold it; the screen renews the code for the real phone. Answers { rejected: true }.

- Input:
  - `device` string, required
  - `window` string, required
- Callers: `deck`, `tailnet`

### `relay.pair.window.renew`

Renew the Wink code inside an open pairing window, with no new proof. It withdraws the previous code at the relay first, so one is ever live. Only the screen that opened the window, while it keeps pinging; at most one every 15 seconds and 40 in a window. Answers { ticket, ticketExpiresAt }.

- Input:
  - `window` string, required
- Callers: `deck`, `tailnet`

### `relay.route.id`

This box's route id and route public key (base64url), for signing into the name directory, and the box's own public key (`box`), which the Wink module hashes into the words a pairing shows. Modules only.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.route.sign`

Sign a name-directory request with the route key. Only a message that begins vyre-names-v1, a newline and this box's own route is signed. Modules only.

- Input:
  - `message` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.setup.begin`

Start a setup session from the code on the install line: register the sealed offer at the relay, discard any earlier unclaimed setup session and its device, and start the hour. Modules only.

- Input:
  - `code` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.setup.end`

End the setup session: drop the setup device, its presence key and its channel. Called at the claim, and by the session itself when its hour runs out with no claim. Modules only, never callable through the setup channel.

- Input:
  - `reason` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.setup.status`

Where the setup session is: none, waiting for the page, paired, or contested (another server used the code first), whether the relay holds the offer, whether the one pairing ticket is made, when the hour ends, and the four check words the page shows too.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `relay.status`

Whether the relay is on and connected, which relay, this box's route id, and how many paired devices and open connections it has.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `relay.ticket.mint`

A Wink ticket with an offer sealed into its record (an invitation: kind, role, projects), or a ticket from a seed both ends derived (a typed code's key). With `gate` (phone or server) the redemption makes nothing but a waiting pairing until relay.pair.pending.confirm says yes (X-1). Modules only; answers like relay.pair.ticket.

- Input:
  - `gate` "phone" or "server"
  - `offer` object
  - `seed` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.web.pin`

Pin the hosted web app to one release this box knows, or clear the pin (empty release) to follow the newest one.

- Input:
  - `release` string, required
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `relay.web.release`

Which build of the hosted web app this box trusts: its release, the content-addressed folder sha and the manifest hash the loader must check. The owner's pin, or the newest release this box knows.

- Input: none
- Callers: `web`

## releases

### `releases.cert`

The SHA-256 of the owner's Android release certificate, made on first use. `vyre phone add` pins it.

- Input: none
- Callers: `agent`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `releases.sign`

Sign the Android release now in the release folder (CI's unsigned APK named by android.json) with the owner's key, so the first download is instant. `vyre update` calls it after it places a new build. Idempotent.

- Input: none
- Callers: `cli`, `local`, `module`

## rules

### `rules.accept`

Make a proposal a standing rule, exactly as proposed. An owner's act, with their presence.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `rules.define`

Make a standing rule. An owner's act, with their presence. A rule only tightens: it can refuse, make an act wait for a named approver, or allow a draft only; it never allows anything.

- Input:
  - `rule` object, required: { kind: never | draft_only | always_ask, binds: [assistants | members], covers: { actions: [exact action names], resource?: urn pattern }, approver?: { person } | { role } (always_ask only), label }
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `rules.disable`

Turn a rule off without deleting it: it binds nothing until it is turned on. An owner's act, with their presence.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `rules.dismiss`

Turn a proposal down. An owner's act, with their presence.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `rules.enable`

Turn a switched-off rule back on. An owner's act, with their presence.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `rules.get`

One rule or proposal by id, with its plain-words view.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `rules.list`

The Space's standing rules (on and off) and the proposals waiting for an owner, each with a plain-words view. A manager and above.

- Input:
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `rules.propose`

Suggest a rule. It does nothing until an owner accepts it.

- Input:
  - `rule` object, required: { kind: never | draft_only | always_ask, binds: [assistants | members], covers: { actions: [exact action names], resource?: urn pattern }, approver?: { person } | { role } (always_ask only), label }
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `rules.remove`

Delete a standing rule. An owner's act, with their presence.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `rules.test`

What the rules would do to an act, without doing it. `as` is assistant (default), member or assistant_for_member; `action` is the act and `resource` its urn. `id` tries one stored rule alone (on or off); `rule` tries one nobody has defined yet beside the ones in force. Answers { outcome: never | always_ask | draft_only | none, binds: [{ id, kind, label, status, view }] }.

- Input:
  - `action` string, required
  - `as` one of "assistant", "member", "assistant_for_member"
  - `id` string
  - `resource` string
  - `rule` object: { kind: never | draft_only | always_ask, binds: [assistants | members], covers: { actions: [exact action names], resource?: urn pattern }, approver?: { person } | { role } (always_ask only), label }
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

## runner

### `runner.here`

The sessions running on this computer now, each with its chat, title, state and what it uses: rows of { thread, title, computer, state, cpuPercent, memoryMb, line, cpu }.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `runner.lock`

Close the workspace on this computer. The data stays encrypted.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `runner.move`

Move a chat's session to the server or back to a computer. Input: thread and to (server or mac). Answers where it runs now.

- Input:
  - `session` string
  - `space` string
  - `thread` string
  - `to` "server" or "mac"
- Callers: any caller

### `runner.pause-all`

Freeze every session running on this computer until resume-all. They keep their place; nothing is lost.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `runner.place`

Where a session in this space would run now: here, the server or waiting, with the reason in words.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
  - `pinned` boolean
- Callers: any caller

### `runner.placement`

Where a chat's session runs now: on a computer or on the server, its state, why it moved, the epoch, and whether it may be brought back. Input: thread (chat or session id).

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: any caller

### `runner.places`

Where the chats of a space run: the sessions lent to a computer, each with its chat, the computer's name and whether it is connected. Only your own chats.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `runner.recover`

Put a session on this server back to its last whole turn before it is resumed after an unclean stop: the provider's transcript is rewritten to exactly the sealed lines (a torn last line and an unfinished turn are dropped). Answers the sealed turn and its state, or nothing when the session was never sealed. Call it only while no process of the session is running.

- Input:
  - `session` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `runner.resume-all`

Let the sessions frozen by pause-all carry on.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `runner.revoke`

The home says this computer's grant for a space ended: stop its sessions and delete the local work and keys.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `runner.settings`

This computer's limits for running a space's sessions: enabled, pluggedInOnly, cpuPercent, memoryMb.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `runner.settings.set`

Change this computer's limits for running sessions: any of enabled, pluggedInOnly, cpuPercent (10 to 100), memoryMb (512 to 65536). Answers the limits now in effect.

- Input:
  - `cpuPercent` integer
  - `enabled` boolean
  - `memoryMb` integer
  - `pluggedInOnly` boolean
- Callers: any caller

### `runner.start`

Start a session here. The space's own definition of the session decides the program, the routes and the credentials it may use; the caller names only the space and the session. Needs both grants and a held key lease.

- Input:
  - `session` string, required
  - `space` string, required
  - `chat` string: The chat this work belongs to (chat_<id>), so the chat can say where it runs.
  - `resume` boolean
- Callers: any caller

### `runner.status`

Whether this computer can run a space's sessions, and what is running here.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `runner.stop`

Stop a session running here.

- Input:
  - `session` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `runner.why-not`

Why a chat's session is not running on a computer: one reason code, or null when it runs where it was meant to. Input: thread.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: any caller

## screen

### `screen.context`

What is on this Mac's screen right now: the front app (name, bundle, pid), its window (title, frame), the focused control (role, name, value, selected text, frame), the page URL in a browser, and the visible text of the window in reading order (capped at textMax characters; truncated says it was cut). Redacted: in a password field or while secure input is on, the focused value and selection are left out and secure is true, and password fields never appear in the text. In a password manager, a system sign-in or permission dialog, a security pane of System Settings, or a Vyre surface, only the app and window title come back, with blind naming why. Local callers only.

- Input:
  - `text` boolean: Include the visible text. Default true; false is faster.
  - `textMax` integer: Most characters of visible text, 0-20000. Default 4000.
- Callers: `cli`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `screen.shot`

Take a screenshot now, of the front window (default) or the main display (window: false). Returns the path of a PNG in a private folder that is deleted after 60 seconds, plus its width and height; read the file to see it. Needs the Screen Recording grant. Refused, with blind naming why, where screen.context is blind. Local callers only.

- Input:
  - `window` boolean: Only the front window. Default true.
- Callers: `cli`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

## sessions

### `sessions.accounts.add`

Add a provider account: label, kind, and for an api-key or setup-token the vault item that holds its credential. Never touches the value.

- Input:
  - `label` string, required
  - `provider` string, required
  - `is_default` boolean: Makes it the provider's pick when nothing else resolves.
  - `kind` one of "api-key", "setup-token", "login"
  - `scope` object: { projects: "*"|[slugs], agents: "*"|[names] }. Default "*": every project and agent may use it until bound narrower.
    - `agents` any
    - `projects` any
  - `vault_item` string: The vault item that already holds the credential (add it in the Vault first and grant it to threads). None for kind login: the provider's own sign-in fills the account.
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `sessions.accounts.bind`

Grant an account to one more project or agent (added to its scope, others it already has kept), or make it its provider's default.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `agent` string
  - `is_default` boolean
  - `project` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `sessions.accounts.key`

Add an AI account from an API key: kind openai-compatible (key and base_url), anthropic-compatible (key and base_url) or openrouter (key). The key is checked with one cheap call, stored in the Vault (bound to that address) and never returned or shown again; the key is sent only to that host, which is looked up again on every turn and refused if it points at a private, tailnet or metadata address. Removing the account removes the key. Only the person (or their own words) adds one.

- Input:
  - `key` string, required
  - `kind` one of "openai-compatible", "anthropic-compatible", "openrouter", required
  - `base_url` string
  - `label` string
  - `model` string
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.accounts.list`

List accounts on one provider or every provider, each with its vault item name (never a value) and the projects and agents it may use.

- Input:
  - `provider` string
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.accounts.remove`

Remove an account. Threads already resumed on it keep running; the next resume on that thread asks for another (a removed account is never a silent fallback).

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.accounts.resolve`

Which account a session on this provider uses, for a project/agent/explicit choice, scope-checked either way.

- Input:
  - `provider` string, required
  - `account` string
  - `agent` string
  - `project` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sessions.accounts.set`

Set an account's privacy choice (Grok only): privacy true is privacy mode on, xAI does not keep the account's sessions and Grok cannot make video; false is off, xAI keeps them and may train on them, and Grok can make video. Records the choice; the setting itself is changed on xAI's side, in Grok's /privacy settings.

- Input:
  - `account` string, required
  - `privacy` boolean, required
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.accounts.signin`

Sign an account in through its provider's own login, no token pasted. Start with provider and label (or account), then poll with flow.

- Input:
  - `account` string
  - `code` string: With flow: the code a login asks back (step url, paste true).
  - `flow` string: From the start answer; alone it says waiting, done or failed.
  - `label` string
  - `provider` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `sessions.environment`

The environment brief an agent starting now is told (what Vyre is, its Space, its records, how to work, approvals, memory, what it can reach), built from live reads and cut to a budget. The same text goes to every model and driver.

- Input:
  - `agent` string
  - `agent_kind` string
  - `artifacts_dir` string
  - `contacts` list of object
  - `now` number
  - `project` string
  - `provider` string
  - `space_zone` string
  - `zone` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sessions.files.read`

Read one regular file from inside an account's own folder, as that account (`file` is relative to the account's HOME, under a provider's own folder such as .grok or .codex, no links, at most 20 MB). Answers { size, sha256, data_b64 }. Not a public name.

- Input:
  - `account` string, required
  - `file` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sessions.harness.get`

What each harness can do: { provider, version, caps, counts, auth, at }. Narrow with `provider`. A harness never started has no row.

- Input:
  - `provider` string: Narrow to one harness. caps are true, false or null (not shown), detected when a session of it last started
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.harness.learn`

Record what a harness showed it can do at session start: { provider, version?, caps: { skills, plugins, mcp, subagents, ... : true | false | null }, counts?, auth? }. For the capability store; not a public name.

- Input:
  - `caps` object, required
  - `provider` string, required
  - `auth` list of string
  - `counts` object
  - `version` string or null
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sessions.limits.get`

The concurrency limits: box-wide (sessions.limits in config: max_active_teammates, max_subagents) and a project's own.

- Input:
  - `project` string
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.limits.set`

Set a project's limits: at most this many active teammates (max_active) and subagents across all its sessions (max_subagents). null removes one. Over a limit, new ones wait their turn.

- Input:
  - `project` string, required
  - `max_active` integer or null
  - `max_subagents` integer or null
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `sessions.mention.resolve`

What an account tagged with @ means for a thread: the person asked this one turn to run on it. No grant; threads.send reads the tag. Called by the mentions core.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `said` string
  - `thread` string
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.mention.search`

The @ picker's Accounts results: the AI accounts that are signed in (Claude, Codex, Grok, OpenRouter), by provider name. Names and a short hint only.

- Input:
  - `limit` integer
  - `q` string
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.mode.get`

The permission mode new sessions in a project start in (sessions.mode.set), or null for Claude Code's default (ask).

- Input:
  - `project` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.mode.resolve`

The mode a new session in a project starts in, for the Switchboard.

- Input:
  - `project` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sessions.mode.set`

The permission mode new sessions in a project start in: default (ask), acceptEdits, plan or bypassPermissions ("Doesn't ask": the security floor and the Gate still hold; only sessions with Vyre's plugin take it). The person's own; default (or no mode) clears it. A running session keeps its mode (threads.mode changes that).

- Input:
  - `project` string, required
  - `mode` one of "default", "acceptEdits", "plan", "bypassPermissions"
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `sessions.models`

The models a thread can switch to (threads.model): id and label, the aliases Claude Code takes first.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.models.get`

Which model each session purpose and project runs on, and where it comes from, plus the model aliases to offer. An agent's own model wins.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.models.resolve`

The model a session starting now runs on, and where that comes from.

- Input:
  - `model` string
  - `project` string
  - `purpose` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sessions.models.set`

Set the model for a purpose (purpose:<chat|agent|project|teammate|capsule|job|memory|planner|learn|helper>) or a project (project:<slug>): an alias (opus, sonnet, haiku) or a full model id. model null removes the override. Applies from the next session.

- Input:
  - `scope` string, required
  - `model` string or null
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `sessions.prompt.compose`

The system prompt for a session starting now: the environment brief, then the levels around Vyre's own launch text, then the project's own context (context, for a driver with no SessionStart hook). purpose "capsule" is the Capsule's quick answer (Vyre IQ): the whole prompt, with append read as its facts.

- Input:
  - `agent` string
  - `agent_kind` string
  - `append` string
  - `artifacts_dir` string
  - `contacts` list of object
  - `context` string
  - `facts` list of string
  - `now` number
  - `project` string
  - `provider` string
  - `purpose` string
  - `space_zone` string
  - `zone` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sessions.prompt.get`

The system prompt set at one level (assistant, agent:<name> or project:<slug>): its text, mode (append or replace) and version, or null when nothing is set.

- Input:
  - `scope` string, required: assistant, agent:<name>, project:<slug> or capsule (the Capsule's quick answer, Vyre IQ)
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.prompt.history`

Every version of the system prompt at one level, newest first.

- Input:
  - `scope` string, required: assistant, agent:<name>, project:<slug> or capsule (the Capsule's quick answer, Vyre IQ)
  - `limit` integer
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.prompt.preview`

Preview the system prompt a session would start with for an agent and project, and how its levels combine. Excludes Vyre's own launch text.

- Input:
  - `agent` string
  - `agent_kind` string
  - `project` string
  - `purpose` "capsule": capsule: the Capsule's quick answer prompt (Vyre IQ), with no facts.
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.prompt.revert`

Undo edits: make an older version of a level's system prompt the current one again (as a new version, so the revert can be undone too).

- Input:
  - `scope` string, required: assistant, agent:<name>, project:<slug> or capsule (the Capsule's quick answer, Vyre IQ)
  - `version` integer, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `sessions.prompt.set`

Set the system prompt at one level. mode "append" (the default) adds it after Claude Code's own; "replace" makes it the whole system prompt: Replacing the system prompt drops Claude Code's own instructions (tool use, safety and coding behaviour), so it is for experts only. Every edit is a new version; sessions.prompt.revert undoes one. Applies from the next session. Empty text in append mode clears the level. At most 20000 characters.

- Input:
  - `scope` string, required: assistant, agent:<name>, project:<slug> or capsule (the Capsule's quick answer, Vyre IQ)
  - `text` string, required
  - `mode` "append" or "replace"
  - `note` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `sessions.providers.learn`

Record what a provider reported in a session: the models its account can use (id, label) and its plan. For providers.list; not a public name.

- Input:
  - `provider` string, required
  - `account` string
  - `models` list of object
    - `id` string, required
    - `label` string
  - `plan` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sessions.providers.snapshot`

Every session provider this module speaks for (claude, codex, grok), each with its own accounts and the models it offers. For providers.list (core/providers) to assemble; not a public name itself.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sessions.routes.get`

The fallback order of (provider, account) a thread moves down when its turn hits a limit, for one scope or all.

- Input:
  - `scope` string: default, project:<slug> or agent:<name>. Omit for every list.
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.routes.next`

The next (provider, account) a limited thread moves to, for the Switchboard. Not a public name.

- Input:
  - `provider` string, required
  - `account` string
  - `agent` string
  - `project` string
  - `tried` list of string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sessions.routes.set`

Set the fallback order for a scope: entries is an ordered list of { provider, account? }, e.g. Claude, then Codex, then Grok. An empty list clears it. Two entries on one provider (two accounts combining one vendor's quota) may break that vendor's terms: it saves only with acknowledge: true, after the person has seen the warning. An agent sets only its own list or a project it is granted.

- Input:
  - `entries` list of object, required
    - `provider` string, required
    - `account` string
    - `model` string: The model this entry runs (required for OpenRouter): what the person chose.
  - `scope` string, required
  - `acknowledge` boolean
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.setup`

Install the Claude Agent SDK now (it installs itself on first use otherwise) and wait. Says how much it downloads. Sessions use it from the next one on.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `sessions.slots`

The concurrency ledger (for the Switchboard and teammates): take a teammate or subagent slot (waiting its turn, or not), release one, release all an owner holds, or read what is held.

- Input:
  - `action` one of "take", "release", "release-owner", "status", required
  - `auth` string: The credential the new session runs on: its plan's usage pause applies.
  - `id` string
  - `key` string
  - `kind` "teammate" or "subagent"
  - `owner` string
  - `project` string
  - `timeout_ms` integer
  - `wait` boolean
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sessions.slots.status`

How many teammates and subagents are running and waiting, box-wide and per project, and the limits in force.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.status`

How sessions run on this machine: driver (sdk or cli), Claude credential kind, Claude Code, idle close, cap, and whether the Agent SDK is installed.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.usage.get`

Each Claude credential's plan usage as Claude Code last reported it (status, window, utilization, resets_at), and whether new teammates and subagents on it are paused.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.usage.report`

The Switchboard's report of a credential's plan usage (thread.limit).

- Input:
  - `auth` string, required
  - `status` string, required
  - `kind` string
  - `resets_at` number
  - `utilization` number
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sessions.usage.resume`

Resume anyway: start teammates and subagents on this credential again although its plan is near the limit, until the window resets.

- Input:
  - `auth` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

## settings

### `settings.changes`

Recent settings changes, newest first: {id, key, level, target, by, said, at, undone}. Give key for one setting's history.

- Input:
  - `key` string
  - `limit` number
- Callers: any caller

### `settings.get`

Settings, each with the value in effect and its source. Give key for one, group for a group, nothing for all. Secrets are masked.

- Input:
  - `device` string: defaults to the caller's own
  - `group` string
  - `key` string
  - `project` string: scope to this project
  - `session` string: scope to this session
- Callers: any caller

### `settings.request`

Change or reset a setting the person asked you to in this conversation. Refused unless their own words asked for it.

- Input:
  - `key` string, required
  - `device` string: defaults to the caller's own
  - `level` one of "account", "project", "device", "session"
  - `project` string: scope to this project
  - `reset` boolean: true clears the setting instead of setting value
  - `session` string: scope to this session
  - `value` any: the new value; leave out with reset
- Callers: `mcp`

### `settings.reset`

Remove a setting's value at one level, so the next level down (then the default) applies again. Logged and undoable like settings.set.

- Input:
  - `key` string, required
  - `confirm` boolean
  - `device` string: defaults to the caller's own
  - `level` one of "account", "project", "device", "session"
  - `preview` boolean
  - `project` string: scope to this project
  - `session` string: scope to this session
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `settings.resolve`

The Vyre-owned values a session starting now in this project should use, as {key: value}. Unset keys are left out.

- Input:
  - `project` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `settings.schema`

Every setting the running modules declare, with its key, group, type, choices, settable levels, when a change applies, and whether changing it loosens security.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `settings.set`

Change a setting at account level, or for one project, device or session (give it). The value is checked against the setting's type, and by its module when it names a check. preview: true returns what would change and writes nothing, with confirm naming what it widens or loosens. No confirm step and no proof: every change is logged (settings.changes) and can be undone (settings.undo). Returns the value now in effect.

- Input:
  - `key` string, required
  - `value` any, required
  - `confirm` boolean
  - `device` string: defaults to the caller's own
  - `level` one of "account", "project", "device", "session"
  - `preview` boolean
  - `project` string: scope to this project
  - `session` string: scope to this session
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `settings.snapshot`

Every setting's value in effect for one surface: values, sources, levels and a rev. Compare rev after a reconnect, then follow settings.changed. Secrets masked.

- Input:
  - `device` string: defaults to the caller's own
  - `project` string: scope to this project
  - `session` string: scope to this session
- Callers: any caller

### `settings.undo`

Undo one settings change (its id from settings.changed or settings.changes): the value before it comes back at the same level. No confirm and no proof.

- Input:
  - `change` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `settings.write`

A module sets or clears one of its own settings (kept by Vyre, no confirm, not loosening security). Modules only.

- Input:
  - `key` string, required
  - `level` "account" or "project"
  - `project` string
  - `value` any
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

## sidebar

### `sidebar.edit`

Change the caller's own sidebar by one step, for example { op: "add", what: "Documents" }. The Space's default is sidebar.team.

- Input:
  - `op` one of "add", "remove", "hide", "show", "move", "group", "set", "reset", required: set takes the whole list in entries; reset drops the caller's own list (sidebar.edit only)
  - `before` string: another entry's key, for op move (or give index)
  - `entries` list: the whole list, for op set
  - `entry` any
  - `group` string or null: a group name, or null, for op group
  - `index` number
  - `key` string
  - `space` string
  - `what` string: a place's or screen's name, for op add
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `sidebar.get`

The Space's default sidebar, the caller's own list, the merged result, whether the caller may set the default, and addable module screens.

- Input:
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `sidebar.pin`

Pin a project, Flow, Connection, records list or saved view to the caller's own sidebar by id. Pinning twice pins once; unpin with sidebar.unpin.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `what` one of "project", "flow", "connection", "records", "view", required
  - `group` string
  - `href` string: a view's address under /u/
  - `label` string
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `sidebar.serve`

A team member's sidebar call, run on behalf of the person and the role the Space's kernel stated. Called only by the home's own sidebar service.

- Input:
  - `call` one of "get", "edit", "team", required
  - `input` object, required
  - `person` string, required
  - `role` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sidebar.stored`

The stored lists behind the settings sidebar.default and sidebar.mine. Called by the settings hub as its store: with no value it reads { value }, with a value it keeps it (cleaned) and answers { value }.

- Input:
  - `key` "default" or "mine", required
  - `value` any
- Callers: any caller

### `sidebar.team`

Change the Space's default sidebar by one step (same ops as sidebar.edit). Owner or admin only; an assistant's request is held for their yes.

- Input:
  - `op` one of "add", "remove", "hide", "show", "move", "group", "set", "reset", required: set takes the whole list in entries; reset drops the caller's own list (sidebar.edit only)
  - `before` string: another entry's key, for op move (or give index)
  - `entries` list: the whole list, for op set
  - `entry` any
  - `group` string or null: a group name, or null, for op group
  - `index` number
  - `key` string
  - `space` string
  - `what` string: a place's or screen's name, for op add
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `sidebar.unpin`

Take a pinned project, Flow, Connection, records list or view off the caller's OWN sidebar: { what, id }.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `what` one of "project", "flow", "connection", "records", "view", required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

## sideview

### `sideview.close`

Put back the windows the side view moved, where they were before sideview.open. Local callers only.

- Input: none
- Callers: `cli`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `sideview.open`

Tile this Mac for working beside a session: the session's window on the left (ratio of the display's width, 0.29 by default, full height between the menu bar and the Dock) and Chrome's front window filling the rest, edge to edge. session: "front" (the front terminal, the default), "terminal" (the top terminal window), {bundle} or {pid}. browser: "chrome" (default) or "glass" (the paired box's Glass page for `glass`, an agent's name or box). url opens in a new Chrome tab. Chrome is opened if it has no window. The frames the windows had are remembered for sideview.close. Needs the Accessibility grant. Local callers only.

- Input:
  - `browser` "chrome" or "glass"
  - `glass` string: With browser glass: an agent's name, or box (the default).
  - `panel` object: Instead of session: the left frame {x, y, w, h} (accessibility points) that the Capsule's own session panel already holds. Only Chrome is moved, fitted from its right edge.
  - `ratio` number: The left window's share of the width, 0.2-0.5. Default 0.29.
  - `session` any: "front", "terminal", {"bundle": "com.apple.Terminal"} or {"pid": 123}.
  - `url` string: An http(s) page to open in the right window.
- Callers: `cli`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `sideview.status`

Whether the side view is open, with its ratio and the two windows (app, title, frame). Local callers only.

- Input: none
- Callers: `cli`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

## sight

### `sight.frame`

One still of an agent's screen: a JPEG scaled to maxWidth (160-1280, default 480), base64. For a small "what the agent is doing now" view that refreshes on sight.stepped, never on a timer; the live view is sight.watch. Refused while a person is signing in on that computer (the shield). The Mac answers local_only.

- Input:
  - `target` string, required
  - `maxWidth` integer
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `sight.now`

What is on one screen now: app, window, URL (without its query), the last step and who holds the keyboard. parts ["text"] adds the visible text and focused control, for mac only; parts ["controls"] adds an agent's controls (never their values). The Mac is never read for a caller from the tailnet.

- Input:
  - `target` string, required: "mac" or "agent:<name>"
  - `parts` list of "text" or "controls"
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `sight.steps`

Recent steps on screens, newest first: action, a short summary, app, outcome, and the thread and tool call that made each. Filter by target or thread. The Mac's steps are left out for a caller from the tailnet.

- Input:
  - `limit` integer
  - `target` string
  - `thread` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `sight.targets`

Every screen Vyre can show: "mac" (this Mac, when the screen module runs here) and one "agent:<name>" per computer. Each row says kind, label, whether it is live, and who holds its keyboard.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `sight.watch`

Open an agent's screen: a one-use ticket for its Glass frame stream (computers.watch). surface names the person's screen, e.g. glass:<device>. The Mac is never streamed: it answers local_only.

- Input:
  - `target` string, required
  - `slow` boolean
  - `surface` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

## signin

### `signin.answer`

The owner's answer to the sign-in ask: { id, approve: true } with the presence proof signed over the card's payload_hash (Face ID on the phone), or { id, approve: false }. A no, a wrong proof or a timed-out ask changes nothing.

- Input:
  - `approve` boolean, required
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `signin.ask`

Ask the owner's phone to sign in this terminal. Answers { id, expires_in_s }; read the outcome with signin.status. Only from a terminal login the daemon can see; nothing is signed in until the owner approves. One ask at a time, open for 5 minutes.

- Input: none
- Callers: `cli`, `local`

### `signin.dev`

Development builds only: make a person session (a cookie) for the owner on the device node you name, marked as the stand-in's. Needs the dev stand-in file and a caller the daemon already counts as the owner.

- Input:
  - `node` string, required
  - `label` string
- Callers: `cli`, `local`

### `signin.end`

Sign this terminal out: its command-line session ends.

- Input: none
- Callers: `cli`, `local`

### `signin.pending`

The command-line sign-in waiting for the owner, as the phone shows it: { id, title, body, op, space, fields, payload_hash } to sign, or { none: true }.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `signin.status`

Where the sign-in ask stands: waiting, approved (with the credential, once, only to the terminal that asked), refused, or none.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: `cli`, `local`

## skills

### `skills.approve`

Say yes to a draft: it becomes the version in use at its level and the one before is retired. Only the level's owner: you for a personal skill, an agent's owner, a project's owner, an owner or an admin for the Space. A plugin with code needs ack: the value skills.draft gave, which is the hash of exactly what it declares.

- Input:
  - `level` one of "space", "personal", "agent", "project", required
  - `name` string, required
  - `version` integer, required
  - `ack` string
  - `scope` string: personal: you (default); agent: the agent's name; project: its short name; space: leave out
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `skills.change.apply`

Approve the draft on its owner's yes. For the proposals path.

- Input:
  - `approver` string, required
  - `hash` string, required
  - `id` string, required
  - `ack` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `skills.change.draft`

Check a draft can be approved and say whose yes it needs. For the proposals path.

- Input:
  - `level` string, required
  - `name` string, required
  - `proposer` string, required
  - `version` integer, required
  - `scope` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `skills.change.title`

The title a stored skill change really has, or null. For the proposals path.

- Input:
  - `hash` string, required
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `skills.draft`

Draft a skill or plugin into the library; nothing uses it until the level's owner approves. Body: SKILL.md, or plugin JSON for kind plugin.

- Input:
  - `body` string, required: kind skill: a SKILL.md with name and description in its front matter. kind plugin: JSON { name, description, skills?, commands?, hooks?, mcp? }; a hook or MCP server is code and the draft says what it declares
  - `level` one of "space", "personal", "agent", "project", required
  - `name` string, required
  - `kind` "skill" or "plugin"
  - `note` string
  - `scope` string: personal: you (default); agent: the agent's name; project: its short name; space: leave out
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `skills.draft.learned`

Draft a skill learn proposed from a repeated procedure, for the owner of this install: at the project level when it names a project, else the owner's own. A draft; the owner approves it.

- Input:
  - `body` string, required
  - `name` string, required
  - `project` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `skills.find`

Skills that fit what you are about to do, best first, as { id, name, level, description, tokens }. Read one with tools_call skills.get.

- Input:
  - `query` string, required: plain words, such as "keep a password out of a file"
  - `harness` string
  - `limit` integer
  - `project` string
- Callers: any caller

### `skills.get`

One skill's whole SKILL.md, by the id skills.list or skills.find gave. Refused as not found when you may not use it.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `skills.hook.run`

Run one approved plugin hook in the script sandbox: no network of its own, only the hosts the plugin declared. Returns what the script emitted.

- Input:
  - `hook` string, required
  - `plugin` string, required
  - `payload` object
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `skills.list`

The skills you may use, each { id, name, level, scope, description, tokens }. Narrow with project or level. skills.find ranks them.

- Input:
  - `harness` string
  - `level` one of "vyre", "space", "personal", "account", "project", "agent"
  - `limit` integer
  - `project` string
- Callers: any caller

### `skills.materialise`

Write the approved library for one AI and one session (ai: claude, codex or grok; person, agent, project): a plugin folder for Claude and Grok, a skills folder for Codex. Returns the folder.

- Input:
  - `ai` one of "claude", "codex", "grok", required
  - `agent` string
  - `person` string
  - `project` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `skills.rollback`

Go back to an earlier version of a skill: it is written again as a new approved version (the owner's own act). to: the version to restore.

- Input:
  - `level` one of "space", "personal", "agent", "project", required
  - `name` string, required
  - `to` integer, required
  - `ack` string
  - `scope` string: personal: you (default); agent: the agent's name; project: its short name; space: leave out
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `skills.versions`

The library's versions, newest first, with who drafted each and which is in use. state: draft shows those waiting for a yes.

- Input:
  - `level` one of "space", "personal", "agent", "project"
  - `name` string
  - `scope` string: personal: you (default); agent: the agent's name; project: its short name; space: leave out
  - `state` one of "draft", "approved", "retired"
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `space`, `tailnet`

## spaces

### `spaces.abilities`

What a person may do in a space right now (a temp's access ends on time). For other modules.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.admin-list`

The finished spaces a person owns or administers here: { spaces: [{ space, name, role }] }, and the identity's own name when it is this device's. For modules (pairing targets).

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.assess-computer`

What it means to keep a space on this computer: it is unreachable while it sleeps, is off or offline.

- Input:
  - `device` object
    - `alwaysOn` boolean
    - `id` string
    - `name` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.attest`

Sign a joiner's nonce with this home's key for a space it hosts: { pub, sig }. For the peer door (modules only).

- Input:
  - `nonce` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.brief`

The person's Spaces by name and role, and which one this home is: what an agent's environment brief says. Names and roles only. Modules only.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.bundle.enrol`

Give a Space's owner's recovery code ONCE so that Space can be backed up with its members, grants, store and sealed values, and brought back on a fresh box with the same code. Each Space has its own owner and its own code. Nothing is stored in the clear: the code only wraps a key the Space keeps sealed.

- Input:
  - `code` string, required
  - `password` string
  - `space` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.bundle.export`

Write the Space bundles now: every Space whose owner turned on backups (it also runs by itself, hourly). A backup of this box carries the files.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.bundle.status`

Which Spaces on this box are backed up, and which are not because their owner has not turned on backups, with the plain line to show for each.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.cancel`

Stop creating a space and roll back what can be rolled back: the name, the key, a new server. Says what it could not remove.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
  - `vpsToken` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.code.submit`

The new server sends the code its person typed (or, for a second server, with join). The two numbers must match, five tries, ten minutes.

- Input:
  - `code` string, required
  - `space` string, required
  - `join` string
  - `vpsToken` string
- Callers: `device`, `relay`, `tailnet`

### `spaces.compute.accept`

As a member: accept (or stop accepting) this space using your own computer for your own sessions. `terms` is the hash the space's terms show; if the space changed them you must look again.

- Input:
  - `enabled` boolean, required
  - `space` string, required
  - `terms` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.compute.allow`

As an owner or admin: allow (or stop allowing) this space's work to run on members' own computers. A member must still accept, and it covers only that member's own sessions on their own machine.

- Input:
  - `enabled` boolean, required
  - `space` string, required
  - `maxSessions` number
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.compute.may-run`

The scheduler asks: may this member's session run on this member's computer? Needs both grants and the same person on the session, the machine and the request.

- Input:
  - `machine` object, required
    - `owner` string, required
  - `session` object, required
    - `owner` string, required
    - `running` number
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.compute.status`

What the space and you have each said about running its work on your computer, and whether it is on.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.create`

Create a space and say where it will live: a server you have (the one command, then a code), a new server (DigitalOcean) or this computer. Runs step by step and can be resumed or cancelled.

- Input:
  - `home` object, required
    - `kind` one of "server", "vps", "this-computer", required
    - `confirmed` boolean
    - `device` object
    - `provider` string
    - `region` string
    - `size` string
    - `token` string
  - `name` string, required
  - `displayName` string
  - `storeChoice` "server" or "cancel"
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.devices.enrol`

Enrol a device of yours in a space: "Add to this device", one tap, nothing more asked. Leave device out for the device you are on.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
  - `device` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.devices.enrolled`

Whether a device is enrolled in a space (true when the device has no list yet). For the kernel and other modules, which refuse a device that is not.

- Input:
  - `device` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.devices.lend`

Lend one of your computers to a space, or stop. The first time for a device in a space needs your Face ID or fingerprint; stopping never does.

- Input:
  - `device` string, required
  - `on` boolean, required
  - `space` string, required
  - `loosen` boolean: Lending only: you mean to allow more network than you did before on this computer.
  - `member` string: Stopping only: the person whose computer it is, when an owner or admin of the space stops it from the space's own server (the computer is then named by the id the space gives it).
  - `network_cap` "provider" or "internet": Lending only: the most network the Space's work may use on your computer. The tightest limit you ever set for this computer stays until you lend again with loosen.
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `spaces.devices.lend.status`

Whether a device of yours is lent to a space, when it was first lent and who allowed it. Only the device's person and the space's owners and admins can ask.

- Input:
  - `device` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.devices.list`

The same as spaces.devices.spaces, for the Device screen: the spaces a device of yours reaches, whether it is enrolled in each, and whether you lend it to that space.

- Input:
  - `device` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.devices.remove`

Remove one of your devices from one space. The space (and the kernel) refuse it from then on; your other spaces and the device's other access are untouched.

- Input:
  - `device` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.devices.restore`

Enrol a device of yours in a space again (the same as spaces.devices.enrol).

- Input:
  - `device` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.devices.set`

Set the whole list of spaces a device is enrolled in, as pairing does: every space you are in comes pre-ticked and the person unticks the ones to leave out. Leave device out for the device you are on.

- Input:
  - `spaces` list of string, required
  - `device` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.devices.spaces`

The spaces a device of yours can reach, each with your role there and whether this device is enrolled in it (`enrolled: false` is the space's "Add to this device"). Leave device out for the device you are on.

- Input:
  - `device` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.get`

One space: its name, home, owners and warnings.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.host-here`

On a server: host a new space in THIS home's kernel for its owner (called by the owner's device over the paired session when a space is made with this server as its home). Answers { space }. Idempotent when given the id. Needs no presence proof: the owner check is the gate.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `acceptBuiltinStore` boolean
  - `id` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.alias`

The DNS TXT record to publish at _vyre-id.<your domain> so your own domain can sit on top of your Vyre name (or a space's). Publish it, then call spaces.identity.alias.add.

- Input:
  - `domain` string, required
  - `name` string
  - `space` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.alias.add`

Add an own domain to your Vyre name (or a space's) once its TXT record is published.

- Input:
  - `domain` string, required
  - `name` string
  - `space` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.code.replace`

Make a new recovery code (and optionally a new recovery password); the old code stops working. The code comes back in this reply only.

- Input:
  - `password` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.contact.key`

As a recovery contact: make the approval key for someone's identity and give them its public half. This device keeps the private half and signs when they ask, after you approve.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.create`

Become yourself on this device: make its key and your identity, and finish the Vyre name that a reservation code (VYRE-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX, made at vyre.run/setup) holds. The recovery code comes back in this reply only: show it to the person once and never keep a copy. A recovery password is optional (four or more words is best); with one, the paper alone is not enough.

- Input:
  - `code` string
  - `deviceLabel` string
  - `name` string
  - `password` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.device`

This device's entry on its identity list: { deviceId, deviceKey }, or null when none is claimed. The public half only. For the daemon.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.identity.devices`

The devices of a person you share a space with: each one's id and its key-agreement point, for wrapping a chat key. Public data only; a person you share no space with gives nothing.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.devices.read`

The devices of a person you share a space with: each one's id and its key-agreement point, for a first-party module that wraps a chat key. Public data only.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.identity.enrol`

Put a newly paired device on this person's identity list. Signed by this device's entry; the device is a newcomer for 24 hours. For pairing.

- Input:
  - `publicKey` string, required
  - `agree` string
  - `attest` string
  - `enclave` string
  - `held` string
  - `label` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.identity.entries`

Who can speak for you: your devices, your recovery code and your recovery contacts, with which are new sign-ins (under 24 hours old).

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.entry`

A device entry as the identity list holds it now, if it belongs to a member of the space: { eid, kind, pub }, else null. Read live every call. For the transport.

- Input:
  - `eid` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.identity.entry.add`

Add a device (its public key from pairing) or a recovery contact (the key the contact made for you). Signed by this device. A sign-in under 24 hours old cannot add a contact.

- Input:
  - `publicKey` string, required
  - `kind` "device" or "contact"
  - `label` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.entry.remove`

Take a device or contact off your list in one tap. Any older device can remove a newcomer. A sign-in under 24 hours old can remove only newer sign-ins.

- Input:
  - `eid` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.evidence`

A person's identity chain and entries from the directory, verified, only if it is the given id's: { ops, entries }. Each entry says whether it is the founder and when it was added (signed time); the door decides what is young. For the home's invitee door.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
  - `name` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.identity.id`

This device's permanent identity id (and its own entry id on the list, `eid`), or null when none is claimed yet. The one place the id is kept is this module; pairing and install read it here, never keep their own. For modules.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.identity.lookup`

A claimed Vyre name's identity list from the directory, verified, and only if it is the given id's: { entries }. Nothing is kept. For the pairing module.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `name` string, required
  - `pin` object
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.identity.name-of`

The claimed Vyre name for a person's id, verified: { name: 'alex.vyre.run' | null }. For modules.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `claimed` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.identity.recover.approve`

As a recovery contact: approve someone's recovery request. Needs your approval on this device.

- Input:
  - `request` object, required
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `spaces.identity.recover.begin`

On a new device with nothing else: ask your recovery contacts. Returns a request to pass to two of them.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `deviceLabel` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.recover.code`

On a new device: take your identity back with the recovery code (and the recovery password if you set one). Works at once; the new sign-in is a newcomer for 24 hours.

- Input:
  - `code` string, required
  - `name` string, required
  - `deviceLabel` string
  - `password` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.recover.finish`

On the new device: put two contacts' approvals on the request and take your identity back.

- Input:
  - `approvals` list of object, required
    - `eid` string, required
    - `sig` string, required
  - `requestId` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.republish`

Put your identity's chain and each finished space's name in the directory again, for a directory that lost its claims (a test server that restarted). Your name is finished again with a fresh reservation code (`code`) when the directory no longer holds it. Says what it put back and what it could not.

- Input:
  - `code` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.resolve`
