Look up a Vyre name (or an own domain) by its exact name and verify its list. Returns the kind, the permanent id and the list's head. There is no way to browse names.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `pin` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.self`

This device's identity id and name, or null when none is claimed. Read live every call. For other modules, so that nothing makes a second identity.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.identity.sign`

Sign the transport's device proof (a message that starts with vyre-wink-peer-v2) with this device's key. Refuses anything else.

- Input:
  - `message` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.identity.state`

A person's identity list as verified now: their entry ids and kinds. Read live each call. For the transport's personOf.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.identity.status`

This device's Vyre identity: its name, its permanent id and its place on the list. No key is ever shown.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.sync`

Check the directory for changes to your list: new sign-ins and removals (each is an alert), whether this device was removed, and whether the directory answered with a stale or different list.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.unwrap-drop`

Open a file drop's wrapped key with this device's key-agreement key: the wrap and its associated data in, the unwrapped file key out. Only for a wrap whose associated data starts with vyre-drop-wrap. For first-party modules only.

- Input:
  - `aad` string, required
  - `wrap` object, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.invites.accept`

Join a space from its link, signing with this device's person key. When this device is the space's home the membership is made at once; otherwise the signed acceptance is returned for the home to redeem.

- Input:
  - `link` string, required
  - `pin` string
  - `presence_key` object
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.invites.confirm`

As the inviter of an admin or owner: confirm the fingerprint words the invitee reads to you. Their invite waits for this.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `space` string, required
  - `words` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.invites.create`

Make a join link (https://<space>.vyre.run/join/...) for a role. A temp or member invite can name projects. Owners and admins only, unless the space lets managers invite.

- Input:
  - `role` one of "owner", "admin", "manager", "member", "temp", required
  - `space` string, required
  - `alias` string
  - `expires` number
  - `scope` list of string
  - `to` string
  - `ttlDays` number
  - `uses` number
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.invites.list`

Invites you made, or all you may manage as owner or admin. Never includes the link.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.invites.preview`

What a join link offers, before joining: the space, the role, what you will see and the button. Checks the link's signature against the space's pinned key. Shows nothing else.

- Input:
  - `link` string, required
  - `pin` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.invites.redeem`

The space's home checks a signed acceptance (from spaces.invites.accept on another device) and makes the membership. The link's signature, the person's own signature and the use count are the authority.

- Input:
  - `person` object, required
    - `id` string, required
    - `by` string
    - `name` string
    - `ops` list of object
    - `publicKey` string
  - `proof` string, required
  - `token` string, required
- Callers: `device`, `relay`, `tailnet`

### `spaces.invites.revoke`

Cancel an invite so its link stops working.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.label`

What a join card shows for a space the kernel hosts: its name and the four fingerprint words of its identity list and root key. With no space it answers for this home's own Space once it holds the root key. The kernel reads this on every invite card.

- Input:
  - `space` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.list`

Spaces on this device that you created or belong to, with your role in each. For a space with a kernel the role is the kernel's answer. On a server that has no identity of its own (paired to yours), the spaces its kernel hosts for its owner.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.members.add`

Add a person (their per_ id or their Vyre name) with a role. A temp member needs scope and an end date. Making an owner needs the person's approval on their device.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
  - `role` one of "owner", "admin", "manager", "member", "temp", required
  - `space` string, required
  - `expires` number
  - `scope` list of string
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `spaces.members.add-agent`

Add an agent (an assistant that does work in the space, for example as the doer of a task) to a space. The kernel's own actor membership: owners and admins only, under the person's proof.

- Input:
  - `agent` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `spaces.members.extend`

Give a temp member a later end date. This is a grant change, so it needs the person's approval on their device.

- Input:
  - `expires` number, required
  - `person` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `spaces.members.list`

Everyone in a space with their role, scope and end date, and any warnings (such as a single owner).

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.members.remove`

Remove a person from a space. A space always keeps at least one owner.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.members.set-role`

Change a person's role. Making someone an owner needs the person's approval on their device.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
  - `role` one of "owner", "admin", "manager", "member", "temp", required
  - `space` string, required
  - `expires` number
  - `scope` list of string
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `spaces.members.transfer`

Hand a space to another member. The old owner becomes an admin (or the role you name). Needs the person's approval on their device.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
  - `to` string, required
  - `demoteTo` one of "admin", "manager", "member"
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `spaces.membership`

A person's membership in a space, or null. For other modules to decide who may do what. For a space with a kernel it is the kernel's answer.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.merge-list`

The spaces a person is in, one entry each: { space, name, color, link }, for a device that merges spaces itself. For modules.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.move.plan`

The plan for moving a space to another home in one action: ordered steps, the checks each needs, nothing moves yet. Owners only.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
  - `to` object, required
    - `kind` one of "server", "vps", "this-computer", required
    - `confirmed` boolean
    - `device` object
    - `provider` string
    - `region` string
    - `size` string
    - `token` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.moves.evidence`

In the SOURCE home: the signed evidence that you started a move of a project out of a space here, for the target to check. { evidence, pub, sig }; the Space's own key signs only what its own log says. `toName` and `pin` say which published space the move goes to: this home resolves that space's key now and holds the right to pull for that space only.

- Input:
  - `move_id` string, required
  - `pin` string, required
  - `space` string, required
  - `toName` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.moves.finish`

In the SOURCE home: the target's signed receipt arrived. Checked against the target space's published key (`toName`, `pin`), then the move is marked done here, and only then may anything be cleared from the source.

- Input:
  - `move_id` string, required
  - `pin` string, required
  - `receipt` object, required
  - `space` string, required
  - `toName` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.moves.pull`

In the SOURCE home: answer one request of a target home's pull (hello, auth, plan, records, file, sealed or done). For the daemon's peer door only: the target proves itself with its Space key, and nothing is served outside the plan this home recomputes under the mover. See lib/spaces/move-pull.js.

- Input:
  - `request` object, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.moves.pull-check-source`

In the TARGET home: is this signature the SOURCE space's, by its published key (`fromName`, `pin`)? The driver asks before it signs anything.

- Input:
  - `from` string, required
  - `fromName` string, required
  - `move_id` string, required
  - `nonce` string, required
  - `pin` string, required
  - `src_sig` string, required
  - `to` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.moves.pull-sign`

In the TARGET home: sign the pull proof for a move this space received, bound to both spaces, the move and the source's nonce, with this space's key. Only for a move this space has received (`project.move_in` in its log).

- Input:
  - `from` string, required
  - `move_id` string, required
  - `nonce` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.moves.receipt`

In the TARGET home, after the copy is checked: finish the move here and answer the receipt the SOURCE needs ({ body, pub, sig }), signed with this space's key over the counts and the root of the per-file hashes.

- Input:
  - `counts` object, required
  - `files_root` string, required
  - `move_id` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.moves.receive`

In the TARGET home: receive a project moved from a space on another home. `bundle` is the signed evidence; `fromName` and `pin` say which published space it names, the way an invite does.

- Input:
  - `bundle` object, required
  - `from` string, required
  - `fromName` string, required
  - `move_id` string, required
  - `pin` string, required
  - `plan_hash` string, required
  - `project` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.moves.source-channel`

In the TARGET home: where the SOURCE space's home can be reached, from its published directory record (`fromName`, `pin`): { relay, route, box }. The driver passes it to the home-to-home channel (`wink.home.call`). Refused unless the record names this very space id.

- Input:
  - `from` string, required
  - `fromName` string, required
  - `pin` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.owner.adopt`

For the pairing module, after it has PROVED the identity: make this home's owner (and its hosted Spaces') the person's identity id. Once only.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
  - `name` string
  - `presence_key` object
    - `device` string, required
    - `key_id` string, required
    - `signer` string, required
    - `spki` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.owner.claimed`

For the pairing module: the identity id that took this home's owner place ({ claimed }), or { claimed: null } while the owner is still the first-start id. Read only.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.people`

The people of a space that hold an identity this device knows: its members and the person of a pending invite. For the transport's personOf.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.person.learn`

For the peer door: remember a person's Vyre name once the directory says it is theirs, so this home can find their identity list (member-device enrolment). Answers { known }.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `name` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.personal-host.set`

Choose which Cloud space keeps your encrypted personal items (Settings). It must be one you are in.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.presence.begin`

On a new device that has lost every presence key: ask the sealing process for the one-time token the recovery needs, for the key this device just made (its id and public key).

- Input:
  - `key_id` string, required
  - `spki` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.presence.recover`

After taking your identity back (the recovery code, or two contacts): give the sealing process the new presence key, vouched for by this device's own key on your list. The process checks your list; the new key counts as a newcomer for 24 hours.

- Input:
  - `key_id` string, required
  - `signer` string, required
  - `spki` string, required
  - `token` string, required
  - `attestation` object
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.presence.sync`

Send the sealing process your current identity list, so a device you removed loses its presence key at once.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.resume`

Continue creating a space from the last good step. Give a new name if the first was taken, confirm 'this computer' if asked, or paste the server token again.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
  - `confirmThisComputer` boolean
  - `name` string
  - `vpsToken` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.retire-here`

On a server: take back a space that was only started here (a failed or cancelled create). Refuses a space with content (fail closed).

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `spaces.roles.names`

Read the display names of the five roles, or rename one (owner or admin). The ids never change.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
  - `name` string
  - `role` one of "owner", "admin", "manager", "member", "temp"
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.self`

This device's person and the space a call is for (the one named, or the only one this person is in), for a module that has already taken the person from the call's kernel chain (ctx.kernel.chain(meta)): `person` is that chain's person, and it is this device's own only when it is the home's own person; anyone else is nobody. For modules.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.server-of`

The paired server's device id that hosts a space this device made, or null. For modules.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.server.install`

The one command to run on a server, the prompt it will show, and the code to type there. For a space still being created it shows the current code; for a finished space it starts adding a server (as compute, or as the new home with moveHome).

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
  - `moveHome` boolean
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.server.of`

The paired server that hosts this space: { device }, or null when this computer does not know one. For the Wink module.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.servers`

The servers this device is paired to, for "Set up My Cloud": { servers: [{ id, name }] }, where `id` is what `spaces.create` takes as `home.device.id`. Empty when none is paired.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.setup.claim`

Continue setting up a space on this device: setup moves here from the device it was on, and the state comes back. Only the person who is setting it up can do this.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.setup.save`

Keep where setup has got to for a space you are setting up (one of look, members, ai, connectors, kit), so another device can carry on. Send setup: null when the last step is done. Only the device setup is on may save; no secret, code or key is kept.

- Input:
  - `setup` object or null, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.status`

Where creating a space has got to: each step, what it is waiting for, and why anything failed, in plain words.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.storage.delete`

Delete one of your objects. With `expected` (its sha256, or null for "must not exist") it deletes only if it is still what you last saw, else answers { ok: false, sha256 } and deletes nothing. Never refused for the cap.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `space` string, required
  - `expected` string or null
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.storage.get`

Read one of your objects: { data (base64), sha256 }, or null when it is not there.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.storage.list`

Your objects under a prefix, one level: `names`, and `entries` ({ name, sha, size }) to compare against.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
  - `prefix` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.storage.put`

Keep an object (ciphertext, base64) in your own storage on a space this server hosts. Refused once your storage reaches the cap the owner set.

- Input:
  - `data` string, required
  - `name` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.storage.put-if`

Keep an object only if it is still what you last saw: `expected` is its sha256 in hex, or null when it should not exist yet. Answers { ok, sha256 }, with the sha256 that is there now.

- Input:
  - `data` string, required
  - `expected` string or null, required
  - `name` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.storage.set-cap`

As an owner: set the storage cap in bytes (0 for none) for one member, or for everyone with person "*".

- Input:
  - `bytes` number, required
  - `person` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.storage.usage`

How much of your storage on this space you have used, and the cap.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.sweep`

End temp memberships and invites whose time has come, and say so (member.expired, invite.expired). Runs by itself an hour apart.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.tier`

Which tier a space is on (basic or cloud), and the Cloud spaces this person is in. For a module that must refuse on a Basic personal space (Planner, tasks). With no space named, the home's own.

- Input:
  - `space` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.time-zone.set`

As an owner or admin: set a space's home time zone (an IANA zone such as America/Los_Angeles). Tasks, Flow schedules and business hours read it.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
  - `zone` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.upgrade.plan`

What moving your Personal space to My Cloud would carry: records by type, what cannot be carried, and the hash your one approval is bound to. Reads only.

- Input:
  - `to` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.upgrade.receipt`

In MY CLOUD's home: say what this space holds of the objects an upgrade carried, signed with this space's key. It reads its OWN records under your chain and answers { body, pub, sig }: the count and the root of the per-object hashes. The Personal space freezes only on this.

- Input:
  - `from` string, required
  - `objects` list, required
  - `space` string, required
  - `upgrade_id` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.upgrade.run`

Move your Personal space to My Cloud with one approval: `plan_hash` is the plan you were shown. Answers what moved and, by name, anything that did not. My Cloud's name, the pinned version of its list and the paired server are worked out from the space this device made (override with `toName`, `pin`, `server`). It asks My Cloud for its signed receipt: only then does this space point to My Cloud and stop taking new records.

- Input:
  - `plan_hash` string, required
  - `to` string, required
  - `approve_proof` object
  - `pin` string
  - `server` string
  - `toName` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.upgrade.wrap-key`

In MY CLOUD's home: answer this space's sealed-value wrapping key, signed with this space's own key, so the device can check it came from the Space it names before it approves carrying sealed values there.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

## spend

### `spend.check`

Whether a provider may spend now: { ok, capped, left, line? }. Ask before a costly call; at the cap, answer from what is known.

- Input:
  - `provider` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spend.raise`

Raise a provider's daily cap (provider: all is the cap over every provider together): to (new cap in USD) or by (add this much), or off: true for no cap. The person's own surfaces only. Takes effect at once; a paused thread goes on when it is resumed, and the cap says again tomorrow if reached.

- Input:
  - `by` number
  - `off` boolean
  - `provider` string
  - `to` number
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `spend.record`

Put one spend in the ledger: { provider, account?, purpose, agent?, thread?, usd, tokens_in?, tokens_out?, calls?, estimated? }. Returns { capped, spent, cap }. At the provider's cap it says so once a day (spend.capped) and pauses the thread it names. For Vyre's own modules.

- Input:
  - `purpose` string, required
  - `account` string
  - `agent` string
  - `calls` integer
  - `estimated` boolean
  - `provider` string
  - `thread` string
  - `tokens_in` integer
  - `tokens_out` integer
  - `usd` number
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spend.summary`

Spend per provider with its cap, and the rows behind it, plus the all-provider total against spend.all.daily_usd. Today in UTC unless day is given.

- Input:
  - `day` string: YYYY-MM-DD for an earlier day; default today (UTC)
- Callers: any caller

## statusline

### `statusline.line`

The Vyre status line as it is right now, recomputed: what needs the user, the box, the assistant.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

## stream

### `stream.catchup`

What happened in a chat since you last read it: the last messages (who said what), the steps taken, questions still open, who joined or left. Plain facts from the chat's own log; nothing is summarised by a model.

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `as` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `stream.export-chat`

A chat's logged frames and member rows, for the chat upgrade. First-party modules only.

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `stream.import-chat`

Put a chat's frames and member rows back (the other end of the chat upgrade); with fresh: true, a chat that already has frames here is left as it is. First-party modules only.

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `frames` list, required
  - `members` list, required
  - `fresh` boolean
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `stream.keep`

Keep one answer of a fan-out set; the others stay, quieter.

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `group` string, required
  - `keep` string, required
  - `as` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `stream.mark-read`

Move the caller's read marker in a session forward to a cursor. The caller's other open connections hear it; nobody else does.

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `upto` integer, required
  - `as` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `stream.open`

A one-use ticket (15 s) for a chat's stream at path, resuming after cursor from (0 for everything the log holds). Also the log's head and floor: a from below floor will be sent a reset.

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `as` string
  - `from` integer
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `stream.open-peer`

Open the session's stream over the Wink peer wire (for a paired device): answers { stream, session, viewer, head, floor }; the frames then arrive as peer stream messages for `stream`, resuming after cursor from. Only over the peer wire.

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `as` string
  - `from` integer
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `stream.pin`

Pin a message in a group chat (on: false unpins it).

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `message` string, required
  - `as` string
  - `on` boolean
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `stream.react`

React to a message in a group chat with an emoji (on: false takes it back).

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `emoji` string, required
  - `message` string, required
  - `as` string
  - `on` boolean
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `stream.second-opinion`

Ask another assistant or model of this chat for its own answer to the same question, on an answer you are reading: { chat, message, to }. The chat shows one short line from you; the other assistant is also given the question, the answer and the last turns from the chat itself. A model the chat does not have yet (codex, grok, provider/model) joins in the same step.

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `message` string, required
  - `to` string, required
  - `as` string
  - `surface` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `stream.send`

Say something in a group chat (a stream session with several people and assistants). The words are the caller's, appended first; then routing decides who answers (an @mention, the default assistant when no person is talking to a person, or the assistants named in to) and each gets the words in its own thread; its replies appear in the group with that assistant as author and the caller as acts_for. Two or more answering assistants make a fan-out set. People and assistants join by being named in people and assistants (an assistant needs a cwd to work in). Retry with the same message id and nothing is said twice. A private message is sent with enc { alg, kid, ct } and no text: an opaque ciphertext made on the person's device, stored and relayed as it is, never parsed, routed to no assistant and kept out of search, memory and export.

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `as` string
  - `assistants` list of any
  - `attachments` list of object
    - `bytes` integer, required
    - `id` string, required
    - `mime` string, required
    - `name` string, required
  - `cwd` string
  - `default` string
  - `enc` object
    - `alg` string, required
    - `ct` string, required
    - `kid` string, required
  - `group` string
  - `mentions` list of string
  - `message` string
  - `mode` "steer" or "queue"
  - `name` string
  - `pasted` list of string
  - `people` list of any
  - `reply_to` string
  - `surface` string
  - `text` string
  - `to` list of string
  - `tz` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `stream.typing`

Tell the chat you are typing (on: false: you stopped). Others see "typing" for a few seconds; nothing is kept.

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `as` string
  - `on` boolean
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

## suggest

### `suggest.offer`

A module adds a suggestion source: tool, one of its own tools (<module>.<name>), and the kinds it answers. The tool gets { prefix, text, surface, context, limit } and has 25 ms; it returns items [{ label, kind?, insert?, detail?, action?, id?, score? (0 to 1), last? }]. Offer again at every start; it replaces the last.

- Input:
  - `kinds` list of one of "mention", "command", "account", "entity", "phrase", "file", "time", required
  - `tool` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `suggest.picked`

The user chose a suggestion: its kind, source and id as suggest.query gave them. It ranks higher next time; the lift fades over weeks.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `kind` one of "mention", "command", "account", "entity", "phrase", "file", "time", required
  - `source` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `suggest.query`

What the user may be typing, at the cursor: names after @ (agents, projects, threads, people), commands after /, and otherwise entities, phrases, accounts and upcoming times for the last word. Answers from cached lists in a few ms; a module's offered source that misses 25 ms is named in late. Returns { items: [{ kind, sub?, label, insert, detail?, action?, source, id, score }], ms, late? }; insert replaces the token at the cursor.

- Input:
  - `surface` string, required
  - `text` string, required
  - `context` object
  - `cursor` integer
  - `limit` integer
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

## sync

### `sync.consent`

Turn a paired peer's session import on or off, on the box's own record: never the device's say-so. Off only stops new uploads: nothing already sent is touched. sync.delete removes what a device sent, as its own action. planHash and included, when the surface reviewed a sync.scan plan with the person, are stored with the consent: sync.delete.import can later remove just that import by its planHash, and every project folder not in included is refused by sync.upload.plan and sync.upload.start, not merely left untagged. The picker's exclusions are enforced, not advisory. folders (Vyre Drive step 4) is the person's confirmed folder-to-project mapping, sync.scan's own proposed slug or whatever they typed instead: the first file that lands for a mapped folder creates that project (if the slug is new) and attaches the folder with projects.add-workspace. A folder left out of folders is synced but attached to no project.

- Input:
  - `machine` string, required
  - `on` boolean, required
  - `folders` list of object: The confirmed folder-to-project mapping: name is one of sync.scan's own folder names, project the id to attach it to.
    - `name` string, required
    - `project` string, required
  - `included` list of string: Project folder names (sync.scan's own names) this plan lets in. Omitted or on: false: no restriction.
  - `planHash` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Registered only on the box.

### `sync.delete`

Delete everything a device sent and everything derived from it: synced/<machine>/ and its quarantine, then sync.deleted. Its own person-only action, never implied by unpairing or turning sync off. Without confirm: true, answers a preview (file and byte counts) and deletes nothing; the person sees the counts, then calls it again with confirm: true.

- Input:
  - `machine` string, required
  - `confirm` boolean
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Registered only on the box.

### `sync.delete.import`

Delete just one approved import's files (those sync.consent's planHash tagged as they landed), leaving anything a separately-approved plan sent for the same device untouched. Its own person-only action, same as sync.delete. Without confirm: true, answers a preview (file and byte counts) and deletes nothing.

- Input:
  - `machine` string, required
  - `planHash` string, required
  - `confirm` boolean
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Registered only on the box.

### `sync.scan`

What this device would offer to sync to the box (Vyre Drive's what-to-sync picker): every project folder under this device's own Claude Code folder (~/.claude/projects or CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/projects), each with its session-file count and total size, so the person sees what is there and can leave folders out before turning sync.consent on. Read-only: nothing is sent, nothing is opened, only sizes are read. Each folder also carries `project`, a proposed project id (lib/project-id.js's slugify of the folder name) for Vyre Drive step 4's folder-to-project mapping: a suggestion only, the person confirms or edits it at sync.consent time; nothing here creates a project or attaches anything. planHash stands for the choice made here: pass it straight to sync.consent's own planHash, so an approved import is tied to what was actually reviewed, not a plan that silently drifted.

- Input:
  - `exclude` list of string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Registered only on the Mac (local).

### `sync.send`

Send this device's own files to the box: sync.upload.plan/start/chunk/finish per file, a per-file ack, and a completion summary (sync.sent, sent/failed/quarantined). mode: "once" sends this list and stops; "sync" is the same send, and the idle-batched watch for new and changed files after it is not yet built (see team/archive/work-journals/federation.md). Only a file inside this device's own Claude Code folder (~/.claude or CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR), no symlink escape, under the size cap, is ever read.

- Input:
  - `files` list of object, required
    - `bytes` number, required
    - `hash` string, required
    - `path` string, required
    - `rel` string, required
  - `mode` "once" or "sync", required
- Callers: `module`
- Registered only on the Mac (local).

### `sync.upload.cancel`

Give up on an open upload before it finishes: drops its temp file and its slot, freeing one of the peer's 8 open uploads without waiting for the idle sweep. Not an error if the id is already gone (finished, expired, or never existed); cancel always succeeds.

- Input:
  - `upload` string, required
  - `companion` string
- Callers: `agent`, `device`, `space`, `tailnet`
- Registered only on the box.

### `sync.upload.chunk`

One chunk of an upload's bytes, at an exact offset. Internal: the daemon's own route calls this after reading the request body.

- Input:
  - `data` any, required
  - `offset` number, required
  - `upload` string, required
  - `companion` string
- Callers: `agent`, `device`, `space`, `tailnet`
- Registered only on the box.

### `sync.upload.finish`

Verify and land a finished upload: checks its hash, scrubs it for secrets, and renames it into synced/<machine>/ (or quarantines it).

- Input:
  - `hash` string, required
  - `upload` string, required
  - `companion` string
- Callers: `agent`, `device`, `space`, `tailnet`
- Registered only on the box.

### `sync.upload.plan`

For a paired peer's own connection: which of its files are new, changed, already here, or outside the approved plan's included folders (excluded, sync.upload.start refuses these too, not merely reported), and its quota. Internal to the device's sender.

- Input:
  - `files` list of object, required
    - `bytes` number, required
    - `hash` string, required
    - `path` string, required
  - `companion` string
- Callers: `agent`, `device`, `space`, `tailnet`
- Registered only on the box.

### `sync.upload.start`

Start (or resume) sending one file: offset is 0 for new, or how many bytes the box already holds for a retry of the exact same path and hash.

- Input:
  - `bytes` number, required
  - `hash` string, required
  - `path` string, required
  - `companion` string
- Callers: `agent`, `device`, `space`, `tailnet`
- Registered only on the box.

## system

### `system.build`

The release's signed record of the web app this daemon serves at /app/: appbuild.json (the sha256 of every file of the build), the signed SHA256SUMS that lists it, and SHA256SUMS.sig. A client (the Mac window) verifies the signature with the release key and the list's own hash, then checks every file it is served. A development build has none.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `system.echo`

Returns what it was given. For checking that tools and the rules path work.

- Input:
  - `text` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `system.info`

What this machine is running: Vyre version and commit, role, host, platform, owner and assistant names with avatar fingerprint8, and network.origins.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `system.modules`

The modules on this machine and the slots each shows in the app: { modules: [{ name, version, state, now: [tool, ...] }] }. A module's Now card is a tool named in shows.deck as now:<tool>; the app calls it and draws what it answers.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `system.rename`

Rename this server: its display name, a label the person chooses (not its vyre.run address). It is shown wherever this machine appears, and a phone sees it when pairing. An empty name goes back to the default.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
- Callers: any caller

## tasks

### `tasks.decide`

Approve or reject a task waiting for a check. The person's own act, with their presence.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `outcome` "approved" or "rejected", required
  - `reason` string
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `tasks.get`

One task, or null when it is not there or not the caller's to read.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `tasks.list`

Tasks, oldest first: by record, doer, checker or state. Only what the caller may read.

- Input:
  - `checker` string
  - `doer` string
  - `record` string
  - `space` string
  - `state` list of one of "waiting", "ready", "working", "needs_check", "stuck", "done", "skipped"
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `tasks.move`

Move a task: start it, flag it stuck (with a reason), skip it, or fix a stuck one. Which kernel act that is, and whether the caller may, is the kernel's.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `to` one of "working", "stuck", "skipped", "ready", required
  - `reason` string
  - `space` string
  - `suggested_fix` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `tasks.request`

Ask for a task: a title, its output, a doer and, when it needs one, a checker. The kernel writes its id, state and who asked.

- Input:
  - `task` object, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `tasks.submit`

The doer hands in what it made. The kernel checks the output and moves the task on (to needs_check or done).

- Input:
  - `evidence` object, required
  - `id` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

## team

### `team.act.target`

Registry only: the destination an asked team call must be said for. Answers { to: [key] }.

- Input:
  - `input` object, required
  - `tool` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `team.add`

Add a teammate to a project: role, brief, and optionally instructions, tools and isolation. Makes agent <role>-<project>; `notice` says if isolation fell back.

- Input:
  - `project` string, required
  - `role` string, required: How sessions address it, e.g. "design".
  - `brief` string: What work goes to this teammate.
  - `helper_model` string
  - `instructions` string
  - `isolation` one of "worktree", "folder", "none": worktree: own git worktree and branch, brings an integrator teammate; falls back to folder (shared with other folder teammates) when the project is not a git repo.
  - `model` string
  - `tools` list of string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`

### `team.ask`

Send work to a project's teammate by role. Queues it in the teammate's inbox, returns {request, state, position}; the result comes back as a message.

- Input:
  - `text` string, required
  - `to` string, required: The teammate's role, e.g. "design".
  - `key` string
  - `model` string: A provider such as codex or grok, provider/model, or a Claude model name; runs this request in its own session on that model, through the same Gate and spend.
  - `priority` one of "urgent", "normal", "low"
  - `project` string
  - `refs` list of string
  - `wait` boolean: true returns the result if it finishes within 30s.
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `team.cancel`

Cancel a queued request. A running request is interrupted only by a person (stop its teammate's session, or team.fail from inside it).

- Input:
  - `request` string, required
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `team.charter.accept`

Accept (or, with decline: true, drop) the charter a session drafted for a teammate: it becomes a new version, written as the person's own act. The person's surfaces only.

- Input:
  - `decline` boolean
  - `project` string
  - `role` string
  - `teammate` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `team.charter.diff`

What a charter version changed: its text beside the previous version's (null for the first), who wrote it and how.

- Input:
  - `project` string
  - `role` string
  - `teammate` string
  - `version` integer
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`

### `team.charter.draft`

Write or rewrite a teammate's charter from its brief, role, project context and notes. Saved as a new version and returned to edit.

- Input:
  - `from` string: A line or conversation summary to draft from.
  - `project` string
  - `role` string
  - `teammate` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `team.charter.get`

A teammate's charter: current version text, who wrote it and when, or null when it has none. A teammate may read its own.

- Input:
  - `project` string
  - `role` string
  - `teammate` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`

### `team.charter.history`

Every version of a teammate's charter, newest first.

- Input:
  - `limit` integer
  - `project` string
  - `role` string
  - `teammate` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`

### `team.charter.revert`

Make an older charter version the current one again, as a new version so the revert can be undone too.

- Input:
  - `version` integer, required
  - `project` string
  - `role` string
  - `teammate` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `team.charter.set`

Write a teammate's charter (a new version; the old ones stay). It adds to the teammate's system prompt and never replaces Vyre's own rules; a live thread starts fresh at its next request so the new charter applies. At most 8000 characters. Person-only: an agent or a session proposes the text to the person, who writes it.

- Input:
  - `text` string, required
  - `note` string
  - `project` string
  - `role` string
  - `teammate` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `team.default.get`

Whether new-work steers to teammates by default in this project (docs/design/teammates.md section 1): the append line and @role's create-on-first-use. On unless a person has turned it off. {project} -> {project, enabled}.

- Input:
  - `project` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `team.default.set`

Turn the default-to-teammates policy on or off for a project: no append line and no @role create-on-first-use while off. Existing teammates keep working either way; this is about steering new work, not removing what is already there. {project, enabled}.

- Input:
  - `enabled` boolean, required
  - `project` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `team.done`

The teammate closes its own running request with a result. Refused if its notes did not change, unless notes: "unchanged" comes with a reason.

- Input:
  - `result` string, required
  - `notes` "unchanged"
  - `reason` string
  - `request` string: Defaults to the teammate's one running request.
  - `result_refs` list of string
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `team.duties.create`

Give a teammate a standing duty that runs when a trigger fires. Needs when and instruction. A person's starts at once; others' wait.

- Input:
  - `instruction` string, required
  - `when` string, required: The trigger in plain words: an event like thread.finished or goal.stale, a schedule like daily 07:00, or a connection's push.
  - `act` boolean: true lets it call tools and ask a model (outward calls still hold at the Gate); false only files what it notices into notes and the waiting list.
  - `project` string
  - `role` string
  - `teammate` string
  - `title` string: A short label the person names it by, like "inbox duty"; shown on the card.
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`

### `team.duties.delete`

Remove a duty and its watcher. A person, the assistant, or a session in the project; never a teammate.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`

### `team.duties.disable`

Pause a duty (its watcher stays, stopped). Open to the person's surfaces and to modules acting for them, because it only stops work; an agent or session pauses through team.duties.update.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `team.duties.enable`

Turn a duty on: the person's own tap. A proposed duty starts its watcher now; a paused one resumes. Person surfaces only (Deck, CLI, Lumen): no module, agent or session; those ask through team.duties.update, which keeps the gate.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `expect` string: The instruction the person was shown; nothing starts if it has changed since.
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `team.duties.list`

A teammate's standing duties: trigger, instruction, whether it acts, whether it is on, and who made it. A teammate may read its own.

- Input:
  - `project` string
  - `role` string
  - `teammate` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`

### `team.duties.run-now`

Run a duty once now, without waiting for its trigger. Refused while it is off.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`

### `team.duties.start`

Turn a proposed duty on when the person's words asked for exactly it; give expect, the instruction you were shown. Refuses if it changed.

- Input:
  - `expect` string, required: The instruction you were shown; refuses if it changed since.
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`

### `team.duties.update`

Change a duty's when, instruction, act or enabled. Only the person turns one on or changes a running one; never a teammate.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `act` boolean
  - `enabled` boolean: true turns a proposed duty on; false pauses it.
  - `expect` string: With enabled true: the instruction you were shown; nothing starts if it has changed since.
  - `instruction` string
  - `when` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`

### `team.fail`

The teammate itself closes its running request as failed, with why. request may be left out; it defaults to the teammate's one running request.

- Input:
  - `reason` string, required
  - `request` string
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `team.list`

The teammates that serve a project: role, brief, state, queue length, last result. Without project, the caller's own; a person with no thread sees all.

- Input:
  - `all` boolean
  - `project` string
- Callers: any caller

### `team.merge`

The integrator's tool: after resolving a conflict or running the project's tests itself, checks both and fast-forwards the project's branch. Refused with the reason.

- Input:
  - `request` string: Defaults to the integrator's one running request.
  - `tests` object
    - `exit_code` number: Exit code of the project's test command; must be 0 when one is set.
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `team.notes`

A teammate's notes, its memory of record. action "get" reads text and version history; "set" writes a new version.

- Input:
  - `agent` string, required
  - `action` "get" or "set"
  - `part` string
  - `text` string: With action set: the new notes text, copied to <project home>/.vyre/team/<role>/notes.md.
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `team.project-append`

The sentence or two sessions should inject into an ordinary project session's append, ahead of any teammate's own thread (docs/design/teammates.md section 1): points new work at team_ask, or nothing when the person has turned the default off for this project. {project} -> {text} (text is null when there is nothing to say).

- Input:
  - `project` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `team.project-has-any`

Cheap check for sessions' own append plumbing: does this project have any teammate (own or shared in)? {project} -> {any}.

- Input:
  - `project` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `team.retire`

Retire a teammate: it leaves the list, queued requests cancel, notes and history stay. Give teammate, or project and role. Refused while a request runs.

- Input:
  - `project` string
  - `reason` string
  - `role` string
  - `teammate` string
  - `undo` boolean: Take back a teammate just made: only while nothing has run for it; removes it and its queued asks, freeing the role.
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `team.role.fill`

Have one of the person's agents fill a role; omit agent to return to the default helper. Notes, charter and history stay; the thread restarts.

- Input:
  - `agent` string: The agent to fill the role; omit for the project's default helper.
  - `project` string
  - `role` string
  - `teammate` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`

### `team.roster`

Registry only: a project's live teammates' roles and their duties (id, teammate, title, hash, enabled, started), for recording what the person asked for. Answers { roles, duties }.

- Input:
  - `project` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `team.status`

One request's state, position and result.

- Input:
  - `request` string, required
- Callers: any caller

## term

### `term.attach`

A fresh one-use ticket for a live terminal (after a reload, a dropped connection or a vyred restart), for the screen that opened it. With from, the stream replays exactly the bytes after that offset.

- Input:
  - `surface` string, required
  - `term` string, required
  - `from` integer
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `term.close`

End a terminal and everything it started.

- Input:
  - `term` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `term.list`

The live terminals: id, folder, the screen that opened it, when, size, open connections, bytes printed and whether each outlives a vyred restart.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `term.open`

Open a terminal: the user's login shell in a folder, on this machine. Returns a one-use ticket (30 s) for the stream at path, and whether the shell outlives a vyred restart (durable).

- Input:
  - `surface` string, required
  - `cols` integer
  - `cwd` string
  - `rows` integer
  - `session` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

## threads

### `threads.answer`

Answer an ask: allow, deny, or always (allow, and stop asking where Claude Code offers it). A question is answered with allow and answers { [question]: chosen label(s) joined with ", ", or the typed text }, or declined with deny. Only a person's surface can answer; a model never approves a permission, its own or another session's. On a box, the person's answer to a paired Mac's ask goes to that Mac (machine: its name, when the box has not seen the ask).

- Input:
  - `ask` string, required
  - `decision` one of "allow", "deny", "always", required
  - `answers` object
  - `machine` string
  - `message` string
  - `scope` "project": With always: allow this tool from now on in the thread's project only (the ask's always_project).
  - `surface` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `link:box`, `local`, `module`, `tailnet`
- Needs a person present.

### `threads.archive`

Put a thread away: it stops, its worktree is cleaned up (branch and commits stay), and it leaves the default list. threads.unarchive brings it back.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.asks`

Questions and permission asks waiting on the user, oldest first, each with its card, asking agent, session anchor and always-allow offers.

- Input:
  - `kind` "question" or "permission": Only questions or only permissions.
  - `machines` "all" or "local": On a box, for the person, paired Macs' open asks are included; local: the box's own only.
  - `thread` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.bind`

SessionStart: bind this session to its claude process, for a key the MCP server sends to say which session a call is from.

- Input:
  - `pid` integer, required
  - `session` string, required
- Callers: `harness`

### `threads.branch`

Branch from any point: a new thread with the conversation up to (not including) a message or a turn, named "<name> (branch)", in the same folder, that the original never sees; taint flags carry over as a fork's do. at: a message uuid or a turn id (thread.turn's turn); none branches from the live end. Refused with unsupported when the provider cannot fork or go back. A person's surface only.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
  - `at` string: A message uuid or a turn id.
  - `prompt` string
  - `surface` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.busy`

Whether a turn is streaming in a folder (a session's worktree) or in a thread's own folder. github asks before Undo resets a worktree.

- Input:
  - `cwd` string
  - `thread` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `threads.chat-of`

The chat a run (or a terminal session, by its session id) is in, or null. A first-party module's.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
- Callers: `module`

### `threads.chat-stop`

Stop the turn a chat is running: one slot's, or every slot's. The chat stays and takes the next message.

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `slot` string
- Callers: any caller

### `threads.chat-switch`

Switch one slot of a chat to another provider or model, between turns: the same chat, the new one given a brief of what was said. slot: agent:<id> or model:<provider>/<model>#<n>, as the chat lists them.

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `slot` string, required
  - `account` string
  - `model` string
  - `provider` string
- Callers: any caller

### `threads.claimed`

Whether a Claude Code session id is a live headless thread in this vyred, and who holds it.

- Input:
  - `session` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `threads.commands`

The slash commands a running thread offers, for a composer's / menu. Send one as a message, e.g. "/compact".

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `threads.contend`

Say a second writer opened a live headless thread's session (a terminal resume); emits thread.contended.

- Input:
  - `session` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `threads.continue-here`

Carry a paired Mac's session on in a new thread on this box: its conversation comes over the link (or from the last synced copy when the Mac is asleep), a box session on the same provider starts with that history as context, and the new thread's id comes back. The Mac's own session is untouched and none of its files come over. A person's own surface only; logged as thread.continued.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required: The Mac session's id.
  - `machine` string: The paired Mac's name or id, when more than one could hold it.
  - `surface` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.delete`

Delete a thread for good: it stops, and its record, questions, queued words and events go. A provider's own transcript file is not touched.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.edit`

Change queued words before they are handed over (re-emits thread.queued with the same ids). Only a person's surface can.

- Input:
  - `queued` integer, required
  - `text` string, required
  - `thread` string, required
  - `mentions` list of object: The # tags the composer picked for the edited words ({kind, id}); as threads.send.
    - `id` string, required
    - `kind` string, required
    - `name` string
  - `pasted` list of string: The spans of the edited words the person pasted. Sending it (even empty) says which words are typed; without it the edited words are not heard as the person's at all, so no tag or ask in them counts.
  - `surface` string
- Callers: any caller

### `threads.edit-retry`

Edit and retry a message, one call: the conversation goes back to just before that message (and the files too with restore code or both), then the new text is sent as the next turn. Idempotent: the same Idempotency-Key neither rewinds nor sends twice. Refused with unsupported when the provider cannot go back; the first message of a session cannot be rewound past. A person's surface only.

- Input:
  - `text` string, required
  - `thread` string, required
  - `message` string: The user message's uuid (thread.turn's uuid). Omitted: the last message a person typed.
  - `restore` one of "conversation", "code", "both": As threads.rewind: the conversation (the default), the files its tools changed since (code), or both.
  - `surface` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.effort`

Set a thread's reasoning effort, as /effort does in Claude Code. A running thread changes at once; a stopped one when it next runs.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
  - `effort` one of "low", "medium", "high", "xhigh", "max": low, medium, high, xhigh or max (the model's own limits apply); left out returns to the model's default.
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.export-chat`

A chat's run rows and their events, for the chat upgrade. First-party modules only.

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `threads.fork`

Continue a session as a copy: a new thread with the same conversation and folder that the original never sees. For terminal-busy sessions.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
  - `at` string: A message's uuid (thread.turn's): fork from just before that turn instead of from the live end.
  - `name` string
  - `prompt` string
  - `surface` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.get`

One thread: its record, its open permission questions, and its recent events (since: an event id).

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
  - `limit` integer
  - `since` integer
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.halt`

Stop a thread with a reason, saying why in the thread first.

- Input:
  - `reason` string, required
  - `thread` string, required
  - `text` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `threads.history`

Exchanges with agents (a send and its replies), newest last.

- Input:
  - `agent` string
  - `before` integer
  - `limit` integer
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `threads.import-chat`

Put a chat's runs and events back (the other end of the chat upgrade). The runs come back stopped; one already here is left as it is. First-party modules only.

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `events` list, required
  - `runs` list, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `threads.inbox`

Words queued for this session, marked handed over (via stop or prompt).

- Input:
  - `session` string, required
  - `via` "stop" or "prompt"
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `threads.interrupt`

Stop the turn a thread is running, as Escape does in Claude Code. The thread stays and takes the next message.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.interrupt-in`

Interrupt every turn streaming in a folder (a session's worktree) and wait for them to end; the threads stay. github calls it before Undo resets a worktree. Answers who stopped and who still runs.

- Input:
  - `cwd` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `threads.items`

A thread as the items a card list draws, oldest first: what was said, tool calls with last state, plan changes, notices. Page with since.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
  - `limit` integer
  - `since` integer: An event id: items after it. The reply's next is the last item's id, or null when nothing is left.
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.key-release`

The Gate's sender for a held message that carried a key: once the person approves the card, save the key to the Vault and send the message with its vault:// reference. Not a public name.

- Input:
  - `content` object, required
  - `id` string, required
  - `to` list of string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `threads.kill-task`

Stop one of a thread's background tasks.

- Input:
  - `task` string, required
  - `thread` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `threads.launch`

Start or resume a thread for an agent, with its credentials set only in that child.

- Input:
  - `agent` string
  - `agent_kind` string
  - `append` string
  - `auth` string
  - `budget_usd` number
  - `cwd` string
  - `effort` one of "low", "medium", "high", "xhigh", "max"
  - `env` object
  - `fallback` object
  - `lean` boolean
  - `model` string
  - `name` string
  - `once` boolean
  - `plugin` boolean
  - `plugins` list of string
  - `project` string
  - `prompt` string
  - `provider` string
  - `purpose` string
  - `resume` string
  - `scope` object
  - `settings` boolean
  - `surface` string
  - `tools` "none" or "default"
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `threads.lease`

Take the keyboard of a thread for a surface. Always succeeds, and says who had it; the other surfaces go read-only.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
  - `surface` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.lineage`

A thread's ancestors, nearest first, up to the person's own thread: the threads it was started for (a teammate's or sub-agent's). The Gate matches what the person said in any of them.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `threads.list`

Headless threads, newest first: who holds each, open questions, and whether a terminal has it open. Running or last-day threads by default.

- Input:
  - `agent` string
  - `all` boolean: Every thread, not only running ones and those active in the last day.
  - `archived` boolean: Only the threads put away (threads.archive).
  - `machines` "all" or "local"
- Callers: any caller

### `threads.live`

Sessions open in a running claude process other than vyred's own threads.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `threads.mode`

Put a running thread in a permission mode, as Shift+Tab does in Claude Code: default (ask), acceptEdits (edits without asking), plan (read and plan only) or bypassPermissions ("Doesn't ask": no questions; Vyre's security floor and the Gate still hold, and only in a session with Vyre's plugin). Only a person's surface can; no answer ever sets it.

- Input:
  - `mode` one of "default", "acceptEdits", "plan", "bypassPermissions", required
  - `thread` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `threads.model`

Switch a thread's model, as /model does in Claude Code. A running thread switches at once; a stopped one when it next runs.

- Input:
  - `model` string, required: An alias (opus, sonnet, haiku) or a model id.
  - `thread` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.models-fetch`

Each API-key account's own provider model list, as { provider, account, ok, body | error }. For the model registry (core/models); never returns a key.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `threads.notice`

Say something in a thread as Vyre.

- Input:
  - `text` string, required
  - `thread` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `threads.of-chat`

The runs inside one chat (its slots): thread, agent, provider, model, account, status. A first-party module's, which has already checked that the person is in the chat (work.chat.get).

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
- Callers: `module`

### `threads.origin`

Whether a session id is a thread this Switchboard started for a person (and on which account), from its own record. A session it has no record of is not.

- Input:
  - `session` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `threads.own-transcript`

The transcript file of a session this Switchboard started, and the provider projects folder it lives under, for the runner to seal each finished turn. A session it has no record of, or one whose provider keeps no such file, is null.

- Input:
  - `session` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `threads.pids`

The processes Claude sessions run in: vyred's own thread children and every live bound session. vyred refuses a person-only call from under any of them.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `threads.post`

Give a thread words from a module (a teammate's result): a turn of their own now if it is idle, else after its running turn. Never steers. request: the request this reply answers (core/team's own id), so a surface with two open asks to the same teammate can match it by id instead of by role, FIFO.

- Input:
  - `text` string, required
  - `thread` string, required
  - `from` string
  - `kind` string
  - `request` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `threads.providers.learn`

Learn a provider account's models and plan without a model turn: start its agent (initialize and a session, which cost nothing), read what it offers, end the session. For sessions after a sign-in; not a public name.

- Input:
  - `provider` string, required
  - `account` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `threads.queue`

The words queued for a thread and not handed over yet, oldest first: queued (row id), uuid, text, surface, at, request.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.quick`

One question to a purpose's warm session (a lean one already started, so no start-up wait): memory (Vyre IQ), planner, helper and the like. A fresh session per question; the system text is fixed per spare, the question's material goes in prompt. Returns { text, ok, cost_usd, warm, ms, thread }.

- Input:
  - `prompt` string, required
  - `purpose` one of "memory", "planner", "learn", "helper", "job", required
  - `model` string
  - `spend_purpose` string: Who this question is for, as a word (for example digest): letters, digits and _ . - up to 50. The cost of this one answer is recorded in the spend ledger under "<your module>:<word>" (spend.summary), never under another module's name; absent, under the session's own purpose.
  - `stream` boolean: Hand partial text to the calling module as it arrives (ctx.call opts.onPartial); the answer still returns whole.
  - `system` string
  - `timeout_ms` integer
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `threads.release`

Give the keyboard back. Releasing a lease you do not hold changes nothing.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
  - `surface` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.remember`

/remember (Claude Code's # mode; # is a tag now): add a line to CLAUDE.md: the project's (project, the default), your own (user) or this folder's private one (local, CLAUDE.local.md). Vyre's own memory is separate.

- Input:
  - `text` string, required
  - `thread` string, required
  - `scope` one of "project", "user", "local"
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `threads.rename`

Give a thread a new name: the session's title everywhere, Records included.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `thread` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.replied`

The turn that answered handed-over words has ended: its last message is their reply.

- Input:
  - `session` string, required
  - `text` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `threads.retry`

Retry a message with the same words: as threads.edit-retry with the message's own text. Idempotent with an Idempotency-Key. A person's surface only.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
  - `message` string: The user message's uuid (thread.turn's uuid). Omitted: the last message a person typed.
  - `restore` one of "conversation", "code", "both": As threads.rewind: the conversation (the default), the files its tools changed since (code), or both.
  - `surface` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.rewind`

Go back to a message, as a double Esc does in Claude Code: the session continues from just before it; its text comes back.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
  - `uuid` string, required: The message's uuid (thread.turn's).
  - `restore` one of "conversation", "code", "both": conversation (the default), code (put back the files its tools changed since, keep the conversation) or both.
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.roll`

Roll a session's window over now, between turns: its agent starts a fresh session in the same folder and the next message goes to it with a seed Vyre builds (your decisions, the plan, an index of what came before, the last turns word for word). The thread and its history do not change, and every earlier turn stays one memory_turn away. Vyre does this by itself when the window passes sessions.rollover_at; this is the same thing asked for. A person's surface only.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `threads.roll-session`

Roll a Claude Code session in the person's own terminal over (`vyre roll`): the seed for a fresh window (their decisions, an index of what came before, the last turns word for word) and the session id to start it under. Vyre does not own that session, so nothing is stopped: the caller starts `claude --session-id <session>` with the seed. A person's surface only.

- Input:
  - `session` string, required
  - `cwd` string
- Callers: any caller

### `threads.rolls`

A thread's rollovers, newest first: when, why, how full the window was, the native session it left and the one it started. A person's surface only.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `threads.send`

Type into a thread, resuming it if stopped. Only the surface holding its lease may type; a free thread is taken on the first keystroke.

- Input:
  - `text` string, required
  - `thread` string, required
  - `asker` string: First-party stream only: the person who asked this turn (the kernel session is opened for them, in `chat`). Anyone else's is ignored.
  - `chat` string: First-party stream only: the chat this turn's reply belongs to. Anyone else's is ignored.
  - `effort` one of "low", "medium", "high", "xhigh", "max": Set this effort first (as threads.effort). A person's surface only.
  - `images` list of object: Pasted images, base64: at most 5, 5 MB each.
    - `data` string, required
    - `media_type` one of "image/png", "image/jpeg", "image/gif", "image/webp", required
  - `machine` string: On a box, the name of the paired Mac to send to, to pick one; otherwise a paired Mac that has the thread gets the words.
  - `mentions` list of object: The # and @ tags the composer picked ({kind, id}), from a person's own surface only; a #Name in the text that is exactly one thing is tagged too. An account chip (kind account, id codex, or codex:<account> when a provider has several) runs this one turn on that provider: it gets the session's memory and files, the reply names it, and the session stays on its own provider. A person's surface only. Refused in plain words, with no fallback, when that account is signed out or out of usage.
    - `id` string, required
    - `kind` string, required
  - `mode` "steer" or "queue": While a turn runs: steer (the default) joins it at Claude's next step, as in Claude Code; queue waits for the turn to end, and can be taken back or edited until then.
  - `model` string: Switch the thread to this model first (as threads.model): the Capsule's Cmd-Return, deeper. A person's surface only.
  - `pasted` list of string: The spans of the text the person pasted (an email, a ticket): a #Name inside one tags nothing, since someone else wrote it; only a picked chip does.
  - `surface` string
  - `tz` string: The IANA time zone of the device that sent these words (Europe/London): kept as the person's current zone for this thread, and said on thread.sent. A person's surface or the stream only.
  - `uuid` string: First-party modules only: the message's own id, so a delivery they retry (core/stream group chats) is handed over once. Anyone else's is ignored; use an Idempotency-Key.
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.send-now`

Send queued words now: they join the running turn at Claude's next step. Not for a session busy in a terminal.

- Input:
  - `queued` integer, required
  - `thread` string, required
  - `surface` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.shell`

Claude Code's ! mode: run a shell line in the thread's folder, as you, under the security floor. Its output shows here and goes to Claude with your next message. Only a person can.

- Input:
  - `command` string, required
  - `thread` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `threads.start`

Start a headless Claude Code session in a folder or project home, owned by vyred. Returns the thread; its id is the session id.

- Input:
  - `account` string: A person's own surface only: the AI account the session runs on (scope-checked, never a silent fallback). A model's call naming one is bad_input.
  - `agent` string: A person's own surface only: start the session as this agent (its credentials and project grants). A model's call naming one is bad_input.
  - `agent_kind` string: A person's own surface only: the kind of the agent named in `agent`. A model's call naming one is bad_input.
  - `append` string
  - `asker` string: First-party stream only: the person id (per_...) of who asked (the kernel session is opened for them, in `chat`). Any other form is refused as bad_input. Anyone else's is ignored.
  - `chat` string: First-party stream only: the chat this session's reply belongs to. Anyone else's is ignored.
  - `cwd` string
  - `effort` one of "low", "medium", "high", "xhigh", "max": Reasoning effort, as /effort: low, medium, high, xhigh or max. Default: the model's own.
  - `lean` boolean: A one-question thread: no Vyre plugin, no tools, no MCP servers, none of the user's settings. Cheap to start.
  - `mentions` list of object: The # tags the composer picked ({kind, id}) for the first prompt, from a person's own surface only; as threads.send.
    - `id` string, required
    - `kind` string, required
    - `name` string
  - `model` string
  - `name` string
  - `parent` string: First-party modules only: the thread this one is started for (a teammate's thread for a person's). A session starting one is its own parent, from what vyred verified.
  - `pasted` list of string: The spans of the prompt the person pasted: a #Name inside one tags nothing. As threads.send.
  - `project` string
  - `prompt` string
  - `provider` string: The session provider: claude (the default), or one a module added.
  - `purpose` one of "chat", "agent", "project", "teammate", "capsule", "job", "memory", "planner", "learn", "helper": What kind of session: picks its model (sessions.models.get). Default: chat, or project in a project.
  - `slot` string: First-party stream only: the slot id (model:<provider>/<model>#<n>) of the chat member that starts this run. Anyone else's is ignored.
  - `surface` string
  - `zone` string: The person's own IANA time zone, from the device in use ("Asia/Karachi"): the brief tells the model what time it is for them. Taken from the calling device when left out.
