- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.stop`

Stop a headless thread. Its transcript stays; threads.send resumes it.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.switch`

Continue a thread on another provider (and account) between turns, keeping its folder and files; the new provider gets a brief of what was said.

- Input:
  - `provider` string, required: claude, codex or grok.
  - `thread` string, required
  - `account` string: One granted to this project or agent; never a guess between two.
  - `model` string
  - `text` string: The next message to send there.
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.tasks`

A running thread's background tasks (shell commands run in the background, subagents): id, kind, title, status (running, completed, failed, killed), summary.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.thinking`

Thinking on (the model decides how much) or off, for a running thread.

- Input:
  - `on` boolean, required
  - `thread` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.unarchive`

Bring an archived thread back into the list; its worktree is made again on the same branch. thread.unarchived is said.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.undo-edit`

Put one file the session edited back as it was before its last turn: { thread, path }. Refused if the file changed since, or is outside the session's folder. The session is told on its next turn.

- Input:
  - `path` string, required
  - `thread` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `threads.unqueue`

Take back queued words before they are handed over: one (queued: the queued_id threads.send gave, or thread.queued's queued) or all of the thread's. Only a person's surface can.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
  - `queued` integer
  - `surface` string
- Callers: any caller

### `threads.unwatch`

Stop waiting on a watch.

- Input:
  - `watch` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `threads.usage`

Turns, time, tokens and cost per agent, and the last rate-limit report.

- Input:
  - `agent` string
  - `since` integer
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `threads.vouch`

The live thread of this agent, or this bound session, that holds this key; or null.

- Input:
  - `key` string, required
  - `agent` string
  - `session` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `threads.watch`

Tell me once when a thread finishes a turn, asks a question, or stops: emits thread.watched, then clears itself.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
  - `note` string
  - `notify` string
  - `until` one of "finished", "asks", "either": finished, asks or either (the default).
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `link`, `link:box`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

## tips

### `tips.dismiss`

Never show this tip again, or with module every tip about that module.

- Input:
  - `id` string
  - `module` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `tips.list`

Every tip the running modules declare, with how often each was shown and whether it was dismissed. For Settings and `vyre tips`.

- Input:
  - `module` string
  - `surface` one of "capsule", "deck", "chat", "phone", "cli", "glass", "statusline"
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `tips.next`

The one tip a surface may show now, or none and why (off, busy, gap, spread, cap, none). Pass the surface, and in context the module the person is in (else context.now's view for that surface), first on the surface's very first open (one welcome tip, once), idle when they have paused, busy while an ask, a prompt or a running turn is on screen (anything in waiting.count counts as busy too). mark: true records it as shown, for a surface that draws it at once (the CLI); otherwise call tips.seen when it is drawn.

- Input:
  - `surface` one of "capsule", "deck", "chat", "phone", "cli", "glass", "statusline", required
  - `context` object
    - `busy` boolean
    - `first` boolean
    - `idle` boolean
    - `module` string
  - `mark` boolean
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `tips.reset`

Bring every tip back: forget what was shown, dismissed and used. Settings' "Show tips again".

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `tips.seen`

A surface drew this tip: it counts toward the gap, the daily cap and the tip's two showings. acted: true when the person followed it (Show me, or ran the command), which retires it.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `surface` one of "capsule", "deck", "chat", "phone", "cli", "glass", "statusline", required
  - `acted` boolean
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `tips.used`

The person used this module (opened its view, ran its verb), without asking for a tip. First-use tips give way to power tips after three uses, and a used module gets no never-used tips.

- Input:
  - `module` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `tips.whatsnew`

Tips that came after the version the person last saw (or after `since`), newest first, for the page `vyre update` prints and the Deck's one quiet card. ack: true records that they saw it, so these stop counting as new.

- Input:
  - `ack` boolean
  - `since` string
  - `surface` one of "capsule", "deck", "chat", "phone", "cli", "glass", "statusline"
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

## undo

### `undo.list`

What was done on the person's behalf, newest first, with id, actor, tool, summary, state and can_undo. Optional filters.

- Input:
  - `actor` string
  - `actor_kind` one of "assistant", "agent", "module", "person"
  - `limit` integer: default 50, at most 200
  - `since` number: only rows at or after this time, in ms
- Callers: any caller

### `undo.record`

Record an action a module took for the person, with the inverse that module declares: {tool, input, summary, inverse: {tool, input}, actor?, why?}. actor is the caller the action ran for (meta.caller of the call that did it); it defaults to the recording module. why holds audit ids only ({said} or {rule}). Refused when the inverse is unknown, outward, needs presence or is the person's own. Returns {id}.

- Input:
  - `input` object, required
  - `inverse` object, required
    - `input` object, required
    - `tool` string, required
  - `summary` string, required
  - `tool` string, required
  - `actor` string
  - `why` object
- Callers: any caller

### `undo.run`

Undo one recorded action by id, running its declared inverse. A person may undo any row; an agent or module only its own.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

## update

### `update.apply`

Ask for the update now: drops the one-line request the box's own update unit acts on, which installs the newest release only if its signature matches Vyre's release key, and puts the old version back if the new one does not start. Answers at once; update.status shows the progress and the result. Where this server has no such unit (a Mac, or no systemd) it says so and the command `vyre update` is the way. The person's own action.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `update.check`

Look at the releases now instead of waiting for the daily look (at most once a minute), then answer like update.status. Off when update.auto is off.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `update.status`

Whether a newer Vyre is out: running version, newest on this channel (null when current), what changed, last check, and how to update. Read only.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `update.whats-new`

What changed in the version that is running, once, after an update: { show, version, from?, notes? }. show is false on a first run and when this person has already seen it. Say it in the notes' own words, then call update.whats-new-seen.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `update.whats-new-seen`

The person has read what is new in the running version: it is not shown again.

- Input:
  - `version` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

## vault

### `vault.account.create`

Set the password for your personal vault. Returns your Secret Key once: print it on the recovery kit, it is not shown again.

- Input:
  - `password` string, required
- Callers: `cli`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.account.enroll-touchid`

Turn on Touch ID unlock of your personal vault on this Mac. Needs your password once.

- Input:
  - `password` string, required
- Callers: `cli`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.account.lock`

Lock your personal vault now. Agents keep what is granted to them.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `vault.account.status`

Whether this vault has an account password, whether it is unlocked, and whether Touch ID unlock is set up here.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `vault.account.unlock`

Unlock your personal vault (logins, cards, notes, one-time codes) with its password, or with Touch ID once enrolled.

- Input:
  - `method` "password" or "touchid"
  - `password` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.account.unlock-phone`

Unlock your personal vault from the phone: its password, and Face ID.

- Input:
  - `password` string, required
- Callers: `device`, `mobile`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.agent.fill`

Sign in on your own computer with a login lent to you; you never see it. Answers which fields were filled and the origin.

- Input:
  - `item` string, required
  - `agent` string
  - `lineage` list of string
  - `origin` string
  - `thread` string
- Callers: `harness`, `mcp`, `module`

### `vault.agent.grant`

Let one agent sign in to one site with one login through vyred. It never reads the login. From Claude it waits for a person.

- Input:
  - `agent` string, required
  - `item` string, required
  - `origin` string, required
  - `expires` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.agent.grants`

Agent logins, active, pending, expired and revoked, with the last use and a use count. Names and origins only.

- Input:
  - `agent` string
  - `item` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `vault.agent.revoke`

Take an agent login away. Needs no one: taking access away is always allowed.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `vault.api.send`

The Gate calls this with { id } once a person approves a held vault.request, and it runs exactly the request the person saw, re-checked. Offered to the Gate as the vault-api sender.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `content` object
  - `to` list of string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `vault.approve`

Approve a pending grant or pass.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.audit`

Who used which item, when, and whether it was allowed. Never a value.

- Input:
  - `limit` integer
  - `name` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `vault.backup`

Write a backup of the whole vault, sealed to a passphrase of its own.

- Input:
  - `file` string, required
  - `passphrase` string, required
- Callers: `cli`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.breach.check`

Opt-in, a network call: sends the first 5 characters of each password's SHA-1 to api.pwnedpasswords.com and compares the rest here. Names only.

- Input: none
- Callers: `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.caps`

What this vyred lets a surface do with the Vault: reveal (needs a presence proof) and the breach check mode. No value.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `vault.clipboard.clear`

Clear the clipboard now, if it still holds what the vault copied.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `vault.codes`

Every one-time code: the current and next code for each item with a TOTP seed, the seconds left, and the issuer. Never a seed.

- Input:
  - `names` list of string
  - `session` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.codes.import`

Bring in accounts from scanned codes: every part of a Google Authenticator export (otpauth-migration://), or otpauth://totp/ addresses. preview stores nothing. A split export waits until every part is scanned.

- Input:
  - `uris` list of string, required
  - `preview` boolean
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.connect`

Fill one module's need: check the fields (or a dropped service-account file) against the provider catalog, save the item with the need's kind and provider, and grant it to the module. For a sign-in provider it stores nothing and returns next: {tool, input}. Values come from a person's surface, never from Claude.

- Input:
  - `module` string, required
  - `need` string, required
  - `fields` object
  - `file` object
    - `content` string, required
    - `filename` string
  - `label` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.connections.allowed`

May `caller` use this connection ({id}, or {source, ref})? {allowed, surface, reason?}. People are always allowed. Ask before acting on a connection.

- Input:
  - `caller` string, required
  - `id` string
  - `ref` string
  - `source` string
- Callers: `module`

### `vault.connections.end`

A task is over: take back every Connection lent for it: { task, reason? }. Only the flows module asks. { ended }.

- Input:
  - `task` string, required
  - `reason` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `vault.connections.get`

One connection's metadata, for the module that acts on it: a row of its own source, or one whose uses name one of its tools. Anything else is not_found. Never a value.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: `module`

### `vault.connections.grant`

Let a surface (capsule, chat, agents or phone) use a connection. Granting agents asks for presence (Touch ID or a passkey): it hands a credential to an autonomous session. Capsule, chat and phone are one tap.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `surface` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.connections.lend`

A task's doer may use named Connections for that task: { task, agent, connections: [ids], until }. Only the flows module asks, and only for credentials the task's approved Kit version names. Use only; anything outward is still held. Idempotent per task, agent and Connection. { lent, already }.

- Input:
  - `agent` string, required
  - `connections` list of string, required
  - `task` string, required
  - `until` number, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `vault.connections.list`

Connections the caller's surface may use, each with the {tool, input} that acts on a capability. Never a value.

- Input:
  - `caller` string: the caller a module acts for
  - `capability` string: narrow to one capability; use is then that one, and suggest_default is true the first time it has two or more ready connections and no default
  - `surface` string: a person may pass one to see that surface's view; a module must pass surface or caller
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `vault.connections.register`

A module registers one of its connections, or refreshes it: {ref, provider, account, auth, label?, capabilities? or tools?, items?, use?}. The source is the module's own name. Returns {id}, stable across calls.

- Input:
  - `account` string, required
  - `auth` string, required
  - `provider` string, required
  - `ref` string, required
  - `capabilities` list of string
  - `items` list of string
  - `label` string
  - `tools` list of string
  - `use` object
- Callers: `module`

### `vault.connections.revoke`

Take a surface's use of a connection away. Needs no one: taking access away is always allowed, but only of the caller's own surface.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `surface` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`

### `vault.connections.sync`

Resync now: the vault's own items with a catalog provider, google.accounts, and mcp.servers with their cached tools. It happens on its own on each source's events.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `vault.connections.unregister`

A module removes one of its own connections.

- Input:
  - `ref` string, required
- Callers: `module`

### `vault.connections.update`

Rename a connection or set its capabilities (both survive every resync), or make it the default for some capabilities (`default_for`; one default per capability, so this clears it elsewhere). A default changes no access and needs no proof of presence.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `capabilities` list of string
  - `default_for` list of string
  - `label` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.copy`

Copy one field of an item to this Mac's clipboard, cleared after 90 seconds. Never returns the value.

- Input:
  - `field` string
  - `front` object
  - `id` string
  - `name` string
  - `session` string
  - `version` integer
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `tailnet`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.credential.tokens`

The connectors module stores a finished sign-in in an oauth api-credential: { name, tokens: { refresh_token?, access_token, expires_in?, token_uri } }. Refused unless the token_uri is the one the credential names.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `tokens` object, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `vault.delete`

Delete an item and its grants. A first-party module may delete only an item it made itself (its own origin).

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.device.approve`

Let a new device into your vault. It receives the account keyset sealed to its own key; a storage device gets no personal key.

- Input:
  - `code` string, required
- Callers: `cli`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.device.code`

A one-time code (8 characters, 5 minutes) to pair a browser extension, or with phone a phone's autofill service that unlocks with its device key.

- Input:
  - `name` string
  - `phone` boolean
- Callers: `cli`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.device.join`

On a new device: make a join code (role full or storage), or finish joining with the approval another device gave.

- Input:
  - `approval` string
  - `role` "full" or "storage"
- Callers: `cli`, `local`

### `vault.device.list`

Your devices in this vault's group: names, roles, fingerprints.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `vault.device.revoke`

Unpair a browser: its token and every session it holds stop working now.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `vault.device.sync`

Push and pull items between your devices now.

- Input: none
- Callers: `cli`, `local`, `mcp`

### `vault.device.unlock`

Open an autofill session for a paired browser without a passphrase, after Touch ID. Returns no token.

- Input:
  - `device` string, required
- Callers: `cli`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.devices`

Browsers paired for autofill, when each was last seen and how many sessions it has open.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `vault.edit`

Change an item in place: merge fields, remove fields, rename, description, url, add or remove hosts. People only, with presence.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `addHosts` list of string
  - `description` string
  - `fields` object
  - `removeFields` list of string
  - `removeHosts` list of string
  - `rename` string
  - `url` string
- Callers: `cli`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.emergency.add`

Keep emergency access for a verified contact: they can ask, and after the wait (7d by default, 1d to 30d) the items open to them unless you deny it. Every item except ssh keys and passkeys unless `items` names some.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
  - `items` list of string
  - `wait` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.emergency.deny`

Close an emergency request (or a release) from a contact. They may ask again, and wait again.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `vault.emergency.list`

Emergency contacts: the wait, where a request stands and when it opens. Names only.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `vault.emergency.refresh`

Rebuild the escrowed emergency ticket for one contact or all, so items added since are in it. It happens on its own at most once a day when the personal vault is unlocked.

- Input:
  - `person` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.emergency.remove`

End a contact's emergency access and delete its escrow.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `vault.emergency.request`

Ask an owner who named you as an emergency contact for access. It opens after their wait unless they deny it.

- Input:
  - `owner` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.emergency.status`

Where an emergency request to an owner stands; once it has opened, the items are taken into this vault.

- Input:
  - `owner` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.env.scan`

The .env files in your project folders that hold secrets: which project, how many secrets, what kinds, whether git tracks the file, and the command that imports it. Names and counts only, never a value. Import each with vault.import and rewrite, which swaps the values for vault references.

- Input:
  - `roots` list of string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `vault.fetch.public`

A person's import of an API description by its address: { url, max_bytes? } -> { status, body, type }. A plain GET with no credential to a public https address (private ranges refused at every hop, size capped). Only the connectors module asks, and it asks only for a person's own act.

- Input:
  - `url` string, required
  - `max_bytes` integer
  - `raw` boolean
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `vault.fill.native`

Fill a login's username and password into the app in front, by Accessibility. The value goes to a helper, never back to the caller.

- Input:
  - `app` object
    - `bundle` string
    - `pid` integer
  - `front` object
    - `bundle` string
    - `pid` integer
  - `id` string
  - `name` string
  - `session` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.fingerprint`

This Vyre's fingerprint; with `with`, that person's fingerprint and the four safety words you should both see.

- Input:
  - `with` string
- Callers: any caller

### `vault.forward`

The kernel's lease module forwards one request from a lent computer's program: { credential, method, url, query?, headers?, body?, session }. It runs here, at the home, through the same checks as vault.request, and returns { status, headers, body (base64) } or { held } for an outward call. Never returns a credential value.

- Input:
  - `credential` string, required
  - `method` one of "GET", "HEAD", "POST", "PUT", "PATCH", "DELETE", required
  - `session` string, required
  - `url` string, required
  - `allow_headers` list of string
  - `body` string or object or list
  - `headers` object
  - `query` object
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `vault.forward.file`

The kernel's lease module forwards one request that moves a file for a lent computer's program: { credential, method, url, query?, headers?, session, upload?: { drive: { path, version?, contentType } } or { multipart: [ { name, value } | { name, filename, contentType, drive: { path, version? } } ] }, saveTo?, stream?, limits?: { maxBytes, contentTypes }, drive?: { read, write } }. The file is read from, or saved to, the Space's Drive by reference at the home and moves a chunk at a time; an outward call is held for a person. Returns the response, { saved }, a stream or { held }; never a credential value.

- Input:
  - `credential` string, required
  - `method` one of "GET", "HEAD", "POST", "PUT", "PATCH", "DELETE", required
  - `session` string, required
  - `url` string, required
  - `allow_headers` list of string
  - `drive` object
  - `headers` object
  - `limits` object
  - `query` object
  - `saveTo` string
  - `stream` boolean
  - `upload` object
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `vault.generate`

Generate a password or passphrase. With `name` it is stored and never returned; Claude must give a name.

- Input:
  - `description` string
  - `length` integer
  - `name` string
  - `symbols` boolean
  - `words` integer
- Callers: `cli`, `local`, `mcp`

### `vault.git`

The git credential helper (git-credential-vyre): get, store or erase, for logins matched by exact origin.

- Input:
  - `action` one of "get", "store", "erase", required
  - `request` string, required
- Callers: `cli`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.grant`

Let a module (or one watcher) use an item through ctx.vault.fetch. From Claude it waits for a person to approve it.

- Input:
  - `module` string, required
  - `name` string, required
  - `project` string: scope the grant to this project; omit for every project
  - `watcher` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.granted`

Whether an item is granted to the calling module, and to exactly this watcher when one is named: { granted }. The same check vault.release makes; no value is returned.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `project` string
  - `watcher` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `vault.grants.status`

Whether each pass holder is covered for what they hold, as last seen at the relay. Reads names and logins only, never a value.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`

### `vault.health`

Watchtower: items that are weak, reused, old, marked to rotate, missing two-factor, missing a passkey the site offers, unprotected, expired or expiring. Names and reason codes only.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `vault.health.dismiss`

Hide the vault health row on Now for a while (default a week). The next Watchtower run after that raises it again if items still need attention.

- Input:
  - `days` integer
- Callers: `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `vault.health.summary`

How many vault items need attention (to rotate, to fix) from the last Watchtower run, counts only, never a name or a value; zero while the person has dismissed it. For the Now screen's one row.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `vault.history`

An item's last versions: when, by whom, and which fields changed. `field` narrows it (password history). Never a value.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `field` string
- Callers: any caller

### `vault.identity`

This Vyre's public card, to give to someone who will share items with you. It holds no secret.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `vault.import`

Import a .env file, a folder of them, or a password manager export. vyred reads the files; values never pass through Claude.

- Input:
  - `conflicts` "skip" or "update": update makes a new version of the existing item
  - `content` string
  - `file` string: path to a .env file, a folder of them, or an export from 1Password, Bitwarden, LastPass, Dashlane, Chrome, Apple Passwords and other managers
  - `filename` string
  - `files` list of string: absolute paths of several .env files, imported under one yes
  - `format` string
  - `rewrite` boolean: swap each imported .env value for a vault:// reference once stored
  - `token` string: the token from vault.import.preview; refuses a file that changed since
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.import.preview`

Preview an import by name and count only: what it adds, skips or finds in conflict, plus a token binding vault.import to this file.

- Input:
  - `content` string
  - `file` string: path to a file, or a folder scanned for .env files (variables come back with type and secret flag)
  - `filename` string
  - `format` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.inject`

Values for `vyre vault run`, which puts them in one child process's environment.

- Input:
  - `items` list of object, required
    - `name` string, required
    - `env` string
    - `field` string
- Callers: `cli`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.item`

One item's metadata: kind, description, field names, url, hosts, grants, ssh public key. Never a value.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `vault.items.names`

Names, kinds and bound hosts of the vault items a person may tag with #, for pickers. Never a value.

- Input:
  - `kind` string
  - `limit` integer
  - `q` string
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `vault.kit`

Print a recovery kit: a one-time page on this machine, gone after one load or ten minutes, with the account id, Secret Key, fingerprint, box address and a QR code. Never the password.

- Input: none
- Callers: `cli`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.link`

Link a vault item to a client, matter or project by its address, so its uses show on that record. Grants nothing; no value moves.

- Input:
  - `item` string, required
  - `to` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`

### `vault.links`

The records a vault item is linked to, or the items linked to a record: names and addresses, never a value.

- Input:
  - `item` string
  - `to` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`

### `vault.list`

Every item's name, kind, description, field names, hosts and grants. Never a value.

- Input:
  - `filter` string
  - `host` string
  - `kind` string
- Callers: any caller

### `vault.lock`

Forget the key until the next unlock.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `vault.match`

Logins for a page, for autofill: names only.

- Input:
  - `url` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `vault.mcp.agent.call`

One Vault tool call as a registered outside agent: { agent, tool, arguments, source } answers { content, isError? } like tools/call. Only the outside-agents module asks.

- Input:
  - `agent` string, required
  - `tool` string, required
  - `arguments` object
  - `source` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `vault.mcp.agent.tools`

The Vault tools a registered outside agent holds through its pass: { tools } (empty when it holds none). Only the outside-agents module asks.

- Input:
  - `agent` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `vault.mcp.base`

The public address the gate serves this box on, or null: { base }. Only the wink module sets it; passes made afterwards name it.

- Input:
  - `base` string or null
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `vault.mcp.items`

The api credentials a pass can share, with the hosts each is pinned to: { items: [{ name, hosts }] }. Names and hosts, never a key.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mobile`

### `vault.mcp.pass.create`

Share chosen api credentials with an outside agent (Claude Code, Codex) through the Vault MCP: it can have calls made with them and never sees them. Returns the token once, and the line to give the outsider. { name, items, days?, expires?, rate?, budget?, hosts?, reveal? }.

- Input:
  - `items` list of string, required
  - `name` string, required
  - `budget` number
  - `days` number
  - `expires` number
  - `hosts` list of string
  - `rate` number
  - `reveal` boolean
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mobile`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.mcp.pass.list`

The passes made for outside agents: who, which credentials, until when, how often they were used. Never a token.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `vault.mcp.pass.revoke`

End a pass for an outside agent: its token opens nothing from now on. Needs no one.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `vault.mcp.reveal.allow`

Let an outside agent see one value, once: your fresh yes, then its next poll for that ask returns the value a single time and it is gone. Logged as vault.revealed-to-pass, never with the value.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mobile`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.mcp.reveal.clear`

Decline an outside agent's ask to see a value (it was never sent).

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mobile`

### `vault.mcp.status`

Whether the Vault MCP endpoint is listening on loopback, and its port: { listening, port }. Only the wink module (the public gate) asks.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `vault.members.accept`

Join a shared vault from an invite.

- Input:
  - `invite` string, required
- Callers: `cli`, `device`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.members.invite`

Invite a person whose card you pinned and verified into a shared vault. Returns an invite for them to accept.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
  - `vault` string, required
  - `role` one of "admin", "member", "read-only"
- Callers: `cli`, `device`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.members.remove`

Remove a member: a new key they never see, and every item they could read flagged for rotation.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
  - `vault` string, required
- Callers: `cli`, `device`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.members.role`

Change a member's role: admin, member or read-only.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
  - `role` one of "admin", "member", "read-only", required
  - `vault` string, required
- Callers: `cli`, `device`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.mention.resolve`

The person tagged #item in their own turn: record that this thread (and the threads under it) may use the item, bound to the hosts it has now. Only sessions and the assistant; answers what the tag grants, never a value.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `thread` string, required
  - `said` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `vault.mention.search`

The # picker's vault items: id and name are the item name, hint says the kind and host.

- Input:
  - `limit` integer
  - `q` string
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `vault.migrate-key`

Move the vault's keychain items to this build of the keychain helper. macOS may ask you to allow it.

- Input: none
- Callers: `cli`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.move`

Move an item into a shared vault. Everyone in it can then use it.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `to` string, required
- Callers: `cli`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.need`

What each module needs from the Vault (its manifest's needs.credentials): each need's state (ready, missing, not_granted, pending, expired) and how to fill it (how, field names and labels, help). A group is ready when any member is. Never a value.

- Input:
  - `module` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `vault.offboard`

Someone left: revoke every pass they hold and list what must be rotated.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.pass.accept`

Take a signed pass ticket someone sent you. From Claude it waits for a person to approve it.

- Input:
  - `ticket` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.pass.create`

Share items with another person's Vyre. Relayed by default: the value never leaves this box. From Claude it waits for approval.

- Input:
  - `holder` string, required
  - `items` list of string, required
  - `card` string
  - `expires` string
  - `hosts` list of string
  - `methods` list of string
  - `mode` "relayed" or "sealed"
  - `note` string
  - `paths` list of string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.pass.list`

Passes this Vyre gave, and passes it holds.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `vault.pass.revoke`

End a pass. A relayed pass stops at once; a sealed one lists what to rotate.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `vault.pending`

Grants and passes an agent asked for, waiting for a person.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `vault.people`

The people this Vyre shares with: name, fingerprint, whether you verified it, and whether a changed key blocks new passes. Never a secret.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `vault.people.verify`

Say you compared fingerprints with a person out of band and they match. The fingerprint is checked against the card on file.

- Input:
  - `fingerprint` string, required
  - `name` string, required
- Callers: `cli`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.person.add`

Pin a person's Vyre card (trust on first use). A changed key blocks new passes until verified. From Claude it waits for a person.

- Input:
  - `card` string, required
  - `name` string
- Callers: `cli`, `local`, `mcp`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.provider.remove`

Remove a stored provider sign-in token.

- Input:
  - `provider` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.provider.set`

Store or replace a provider's session sign-in token (for Claude, the one `claude setup-token` makes). Sealed, yours, never shown again; the session launcher is the only thing that receives it.

- Input:
  - `provider` string, required
  - `token` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.provider.status`

Which provider sign-in tokens are stored and when each was added. Never the value.

- Input:
  - `provider` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `vault.put`

Add or replace an item. Values come from `vyre vault put`'s hidden prompt or a module, never from Claude.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `apps` list of string
  - `description` string
  - `details` object
    - `address` string
    - `count` integer
    - `credential` string
    - `expires` integer or string
    - `filename` string
    - `issuer` string
    - `product` string
    - `provider` string
    - `rp` string
    - `scope` list of string
    - `ssid` string
  - `fields` object
  - `grants` list of string
  - `hosts` list of string
  - `kind` one of "login", "authenticator", "passkey", "card", "address", "identity", "note", "api-key", "pat", "oauth", "cloud", "db-url", "secret", "env-set", "ssh-key", "cert", "recovery-codes", "wifi", "license", "file", "api-credential"
  - `relay` object
    - `body` boolean
  - `reprompt` boolean
  - `url` string
  - `value` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`, `module`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.relay`

Use an item someone relayed to you: put {{vault}} (or {{vault.<field>}}) in a header or the body, and their Vyre adds the value.

- Input:
  - `item` string, required
  - `request` object, required
    - `url` string, required
    - `body` string
    - `headers` object
    - `method` string
  - `owner` string
- Callers: `cli`, `local`, `module`

### `vault.release`

One value, to a module holding a grant for it, or to Publish for a deployment holding its own grant. `project`, when the grant names one, must match.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `deployment` string
  - `field` string
  - `project` string
  - `watcher` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `vault.remind.run`

Run the daily reminder pass now: new Watchtower findings become planner todos in the Vault list, fixed ones are marked done. Names only.

- Input: none
- Callers: `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `vault.render`

Render a template's {{ vault://item/field }} references into a file vyred writes itself (0600), so values never cross the socket.

- Input:
  - `out` string, required
  - `template` string, required
  - `force` boolean
- Callers: `cli`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.request`

HTTP call to a vendor API using a vault api-credential; Vyre adds the key. Reads run at once; sends, payments, deletes wait at the Gate.

- Input:
  - `credential` string, required: name of an api-credential in the vault
  - `body` string or object or list
  - `headers` object
  - `input` object
  - `method` one of "GET", "HEAD", "POST", "PUT", "PATCH", "DELETE"
  - `operation` string
  - `query` object
  - `url` string
  - `watcher` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `vault.resolve`

Values for vault://item/field references, for `vyre vault read` and `inject` to stdout. People only, with presence.

- Input:
  - `refs` list of string, required
  - `destination` string
- Callers: `cli`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.restore`

Restore a backup. merge adds what is missing; replace needs an empty vault.

- Input:
  - `file` string, required
  - `passphrase` string, required
  - `mode` "merge" or "replace"
- Callers: `cli`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.reveal`

Show one field of an item to the person, on their own device. Hide it again after concealAfter seconds.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `field` string
  - `session` string
  - `version` integer
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `tailnet`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.revert`

Put an older version of an item back, as a new version.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `version` integer, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.revoke`

Take an item away from a module, or from one of its watchers, in one project or (with no project) every one.

- Input:
  - `module` string, required
  - `name` string, required
  - `project` string
  - `watcher` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `vault.rotate`

Rotate an item at its provider: make the new credential with the current one, store it as a new version, then revoke the old one. For a provider without an API, returns its page and steps instead.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.rotation`

How an item is rotated: automatically through its provider's API, or by hand at the provider's page with the steps. Names only.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`

### `vault.said.add`

The person adds a standing permission from a person surface (Settings). Only the Gate calls it, after it has checked the caller is the person's own surface.

- Input:
  - `kind` one of "send", "post", "pay", "act_out", "setting", "revoke", "use", required
  - `surface` string, required
  - `to` list of string, required
  - `agents` list of string
  - `channel` string
  - `limits` object
    - `currency` string
    - `max_amount` number
  - `what` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `vault.said.list`

The intents the person has voiced, live ones unless `all`; the Gate's gate.said.list shows them to the person.

- Input:
  - `all` boolean
  - `thread` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `vault.said.match`

Whether an outward call is covered by something the person said before it: kind, channel and every recipient exact, pay by payee and amount within limits. { matched, id? }. The Gate asks before it holds.

- Input:
  - `kind` string, required
  - `agent` string
  - `amount` number
  - `at` integer
  - `channel` string
  - `consume` boolean
  - `currency` string
  - `lineage` list of string
  - `payee` string
  - `thread` string
  - `to` list of string
  - `via` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `vault.said.record`

Store what the person's own turn asked for. Only sessions and the assistant call it, after extracting it from a `said` row; nothing a model, agent, watcher or tool result produces can.

- Input:
  - `kind` one of "send", "post", "pay", "act_out", "setting", "revoke", "use", required
  - `said` string, required
  - `thread` string, required
  - `what` string, required
  - `agents` list of string
  - `at` integer
  - `channel` string
  - `limits` object
    - `currency` string
    - `hosts` list of string
    - `max_amount` number
  - `standing` boolean
  - `to` list of string
  - `when` string
  - `window_minutes` number
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `vault.said.revoke`

Take an intent back. The Gate's gate.said.revoke calls this for the person; no proof of presence, since removing permission never needs one.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `vault.search`

Items whose name, description, kind or hosts match, for a launcher's results. Names only, never a value.

- Input:
  - `limit` integer
  - `q` string
- Callers: any caller

### `vault.service.catalog`

The connectors a Flow may call: { connectors: { <name>: { allow: [{ method?, path }], deny: [{ method?, path }], draft?, idempotency?, rate?, ops? } } }, the credential's own `service` rules and what a declared connector says beside them (the draft op, its idempotency header, its rate and each op's outward flag and read-back). No host, no secret. Only the kernel's lease module asks.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `vault.service.forward`

A Flow's call through a connector: { connector, request: { method, path, query?, headers?, body?, upload?, saveTo? }, idem?, approval? }. Refused unless the connector's `service` rules allow the method and path. Returns { status, ok, headers, body (base64) }, or { saved } for a Drive save, or { held } when the vault itself must ask. Only the kernel's lease module calls it, after authorizing the caller's chain; `approval` is an ask-first task id the kernel has already seen approved.

- Input:
  - `connector` string, required
  - `request` object, required
  - `approval` string
  - `idem` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `vault.session.close`

Lock a surface's session now.

- Input:
  - `session` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `vault.session.open`

Unlock the vault in the Deck, the Capsule or the extension for a while. Returns a session token for that surface only.

- Input:
  - `surface` one of "deck", "capsule", "extension", required
  - `ttl_s` integer
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `tailnet`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.session.status`

Whether a session is unlocked, until when, and for which surface.

- Input:
  - `session` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `tailnet`

### `vault.ssh.add`

Add an existing OpenSSH private key file. vyred reads the file itself; the key never crosses the socket.

- Input:
  - `file` string, required
  - `name` string, required
- Callers: `cli`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.ssh.approvals`

SSH signing leases (key, destination host, until) and requests waiting for a person.

- Input: none
- Callers: `cli`, `local`

### `vault.ssh.approve`

Approve a waiting SSH signing request: its key may sign for that host for 8 hours or until the vault locks.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: `cli`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.ssh.forget`

End SSH signing leases: all of them, or those for one key or host. The next signature asks again.

- Input:
  - `host` string
  - `name` string
- Callers: any caller

### `vault.ssh.generate`

Generate an SSH key in vyred and store it; returns only the public key and fingerprint. A new name only.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `comment` string
  - `description` string
  - `type` one of "ed25519", "rsa", "ecdsa"
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`

### `vault.ssh.keys`

SSH keys in the vault: name, type, fingerprint and public key, plus the agent socket. Never a private key.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `vault.state`

Whether the vault is open, for the app's empty and locked states: { locked, keystore, unlock: "passphrase" | "none", items }. Never a value.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `vault.sweep`

Search a folder, its git history and the shell history for values the vault holds and credentials it lacks. Returns places and names, never values.

- Input:
  - `path` string, required
  - `history` boolean: also search the folder's git history
  - `shell` boolean: also search the shell history
- Callers: `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.tagged`

Conversations a # tag has lent a login to: { tags: [{ id, item, thread, hosts, since, lastUsed }] }. Never a value.

- Input:
  - `item` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `tailnet`

### `vault.totp`

The current one-time code for a login with a TOTP seed.

- Input:
  - `id` string
  - `name` string
  - `session` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `module`, `tailnet`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.unlink`

Take a vault item's link to a record away. The item and the record stay.

- Input:
  - `item` string, required
  - `to` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`

### `vault.unlock`

Unlock a passphrase vault (the first unlock sets the passphrase).

- Input:
  - `passphrase` string, required
- Callers: `cli`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.unlock-passphrase`

Set the passphrase a paired browser types to unlock autofill. Changing it ends every session.

- Input:
  - `passphrase` string, required
- Callers: `cli`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.untag`

Take a login back from a conversation a # tag lent it to. Needs no proof: taking access away is always allowed.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `tailnet`

### `vault.update`

Add or change an item by merging fields: only the fields given are replaced, `remove` drops fields, and `generate` makes a new value on this machine that is never returned.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `description` string
  - `details` object
    - `address` string
    - `count` integer
    - `credential` string
    - `expires` integer or string
    - `filename` string
    - `issuer` string
    - `product` string
    - `provider` string
    - `rp` string
    - `scope` list of string
    - `ssid` string
  - `fields` object
  - `generate` object
    - `field` string
    - `length` integer
    - `symbols` boolean
    - `words` integer
  - `hosts` list of string
  - `kind` one of "login", "authenticator", "passkey", "card", "address", "identity", "note", "api-key", "pat", "oauth", "cloud", "db-url", "secret", "env-set", "ssh-key", "cert", "recovery-codes", "wifi", "license", "file", "api-credential"
  - `remove` list of string
  - `url` string
- Callers: `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.use.check`

Whether a thread (or a thread it descends from) may use a vault item because the person tagged it. { item, thread, lineage?, hosts? } -> { allowed, id? }. Never a value. A host the item has now that it did not have at the tag ends the permission.

- Input:
  - `hosts` list of string, required
  - `item` string, required
  - `thread` string, required
  - `lineage` list of string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `vault.use.note`

A quiet record that a tagged item was used: emits vault.used {item, thread, via} with no value.

- Input:
  - `item` string, required
  - `thread` string
  - `via` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `vault.used-by`

Everything that uses one credential: modules, Connections, agents, sites, apps, Flows and linked records, each with what a new value does to it. Names, never a value.

- Input:
  - `item` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`

### `vault.uses`

Every use of an item: when, which item, which agent or device, which origin and surface, and whether it was allowed. Never a value.

- Input:
  - `agent` string
  - `item` string
  - `limit` integer
  - `since` any: ms since epoch or an ISO date
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `vault.uses.for`

The recent uses of the items linked to one record, newest first, in plain words: which item, when, by whom. Never a value.

- Input:
  - `urn` string, required
  - `limit` integer
- Callers: `module`

### `vault.vaults.create`

Make a shared vault. This Vyre is its owner and its home.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
- Callers: `cli`, `device`, `local`

### `vault.vaults.list`

Shared vaults: members, roles, fingerprints and item names. Never a value.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `vault.vaults.rotate`

Give a shared vault a new key. Members keep access; item keys are re-wrapped.

- Input:
  - `vault` string, required
- Callers: `cli`, `device`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `vault.vaults.sync`

Pull what changed in shared vaults from their homes.

- Input:
  - `vault` string
- Callers: `cli`, `device`, `local`, `mcp`

### `vault.verify`

vault.pending

- Input: not known. The module did not register this tool when started without a live box, so its schema could not be read.
- Callers: any caller

## views

### `views.act`

What an action does: { v: 1, kind: "done" | "held" | "needs" | "error" | "view" | "preview" | "push", ... }. A `do` effect (open, copy, say, ask) is returned for the Capsule to carry out; a tool action calls the module's own tool. An outward action first answers a preview with the exact words and a hash; the same call with `asked: { hash }` sends. Nothing is sent until the person's second Enter.

- Input:
  - `action` string, required
  - `command` string, required
  - `module` string, required
  - `asked` object
  - `block` string
  - `column` string
  - `fields` object
  - `form` string
  - `front` object
  - `id` string
  - `q` string
  - `surface` one of "app", "phone", "chat", "lumen"
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `views.get`

One frame for a command: { v: 1, kind: "list" | "board" | "summary" | "detail" | "form" | "error" | "needs" | "held", ... }. `view` is list (default), detail (with id) or form (with form). Rows carry action ids, never tool names; text is data.

- Input:
  - `command` string, required
  - `module` string, required
  - `block` string
  - `cursor` string
  - `form` string
  - `id` string
  - `q` string
  - `surface` one of "app", "phone", "chat", "lumen"
  - `view` one of "list", "detail", "form", "board", "summary"
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `views.list`

Every command the running modules declare for the Capsule, titles and keywords only: [{ module, id, title, keywords, alias, icon, root, arg, taggable, firstParty, hash }]. Sorted by title. An added module's commands carry firstParty false, and the Capsule marks its rows "from <module>"; its root is off until the person turns it on.

- Input: none
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `views.preview`

A screen that is not installed anywhere yet, resolved the way views.get would: { screen, surface? } -> the v2 screen with each block's data read (as the asking person), for the owner to see a proposal before they say yes.

- Input:
  - `screen` object, required
  - `q` string
  - `surface` one of "app", "phone", "chat", "lumen"
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `views.show`

Show a module screen in this chat as a small read-only card. `module` and `command` come from views.list; `id` opens one record.

- Input:
  - `command` string, required
  - `module` string, required
  - `id` string
  - `q` string
  - `thread` string
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `views.shown`

The screens the assistant showed in a thread, oldest first: [{ module, command, id?, title, at }]. The chat draws each as a card by asking views.get for its chat form.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `space`, `tailnet`

## vitals

### `vitals.advice`

Sizing suggestions from the last 30 days of hourly rollups (for example, RAM at 90% or more on several days this week). Read on demand only, never pushed. Person-level only.

- Input:
  - `device` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`, `tailnet`

### `vitals.explain`

A compact digest for IQ: the top consumer over the last 15 minutes, any open trouble episode, and the trend. Person-level only.

- Input:
  - `device` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`, `tailnet`

### `vitals.status`

Full detail for one device: its latest sample, 24h minute history, and (on the server) each running computer's own breakdown. Person-level only.

- Input:
  - `device` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`, `tailnet`

### `vitals.summary`

Aggregate numbers only, never process names or window titles: this device's latest sample, or an agent's own computer. Any caller.

- Input:
  - `device` string
- Callers: any caller

### `vitals.watch`

Open or close one live subscription to this device's vitals, at ~2s while at least one is open. Answers the current sample and the in-memory sparkline buffer.

- Input:
  - `action` "open" or "close", required
  - `device` string
  - `scope` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`, `tailnet`

## voice

### `voice.listen`

A one-use ticket (30 s) for the WebSocket at path: /v1/streams/voice/listen?ticket=<t>, the same shape as term.attach. For a caller that cannot set x-vyre-caller itself (the Deck's browser WebSocket); the native Capsule may still connect directly.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `voice.settings`

Change the speech provider, whether replies are spoken, or the voice. The key itself is saved through the Vault, never here.

- Input:
  - `provider` one of "deepgram", "openai", "elevenlabs"
  - `speak` boolean
  - `voice` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `voice.speak`

Say a reply aloud through the speech provider. Pass reply: true for the assistant's written reply; it is made speakable first (no code, tables or links, cut at a sentence). A surface calls it when the person's own question was spoken, never for a typed one. Returns a one-time ticket; GET /v1/voice/speech?ticket= on vyred's socket streams the audio (audio/mpeg) to the caller holding it.

- Input:
  - `text` string, required
  - `reply` boolean: The text is a written reply: code, tables and links are left out, and a long one is cut at a sentence with a note that the rest is on screen.
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `voice.status`

The speech provider, whether its key is saved (never the key), whether replies are spoken, whether the provider can be reached right now, and the path of the built mic helper (null until local/voice/build.sh has run).

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

## vyre

### `vyre.core`

Vyre's modules, live, each with name, group, folder, runs_on, does and state (running, stopped, failed). For a module's tools ask tools_find.

- Input:
  - `module` string: Returns just this module.
  - `query` string: Keeps the modules whose name or purpose has these words.
- Callers: any caller

## waiting

### `waiting.count`

How many things wait on the user, and how many of each kind (approval, ask, draft, access, run, task, eval, reminder, pairing).

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `waiting.list`

Everything waiting on the user, newest first: yes waiting on the phone (approval), session asks (ask), held drafts (draft), the vault's pending requests (access), Flow runs that stopped or are stuck (run), stuck tasks (task), a new model's evals waiting for a yes (eval), ringing reminders (reminder) and pairing requests (pairing). The asks, drafts and vault requests are read from the approvals queue. Each row: id, kind, title, detail?, project?, thread?, at, source, and answer {tool, input, fill}: the owner's tool that settles it, the input it already has, and what the person still gives. Also count and by_kind over all rows, and partial: the sources that could not be read.

- Input:
  - `limit` integer
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

## watchers

### `watchers.card`

What to show before a watcher is turned on: when, check and do lines, what it reads, whether it acts, its cost, and the hash.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `watchers.create`

Turn on a watcher as last dry-run, with the card's hash. Runs once now, then on schedule. A model needs the person's say-so.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `hash` string: the card's hash, from watchers.card
- Callers: any caller

### `watchers.create.target`

For the gate: the key watchers.create acts on, watchers.create:<project>/<name>@<hash> of the code now in the folder; no hash or a different hash answers nothing.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `watchers.delete`

Stop and forget a watcher; a duty's folder goes too and its filed items stay.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `watchers.duty.create`

Create and turn on a teammate's standing duty: name duty-<role>-<id>, project, owner {kind: teammate, teammate}, when (an event like thread.finished, a schedule like daily 07:00, or push gmail), instruction, act. The teammates module's call, for a duty a person turned on.

- Input:
  - `instruction` string, required
  - `name` string, required
  - `owner` object, required
  - `project` string, required
  - `when` string, required
  - `act` boolean
- Callers: any caller

### `watchers.duty.delete`

Stop and forget a teammate's duty; its folder goes and its filed items stay. The teammates module's call.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `watchers.duty.resume`

Resume a paused duty of a teammate that a person turned on. The teammates module's call.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `hash` string
- Callers: any caller

### `watchers.duty.run`

Run a teammate's turned-on duty now and return what happened. The teammates module's call.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `watchers.duty.update`

Change a teammate's duty: when, instruction or act. It keeps its cursor and stays on or paused as it was. The teammates module's call.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `act` boolean
  - `instruction` string
  - `when` string
- Callers: any caller

### `watchers.hook`

A webhook call for a watcher whose schedule is webhook.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `body` any
  - `token` string
- Callers: its webhook route only, `POST /v1/<module>/<name>/hook`

### `watchers.hook.run`

Run one approved plugin hook's script once in the watchers' sandbox: no credentials, only the declared hosts. The skills module's call.

- Input:
  - `script` string, required
  - `hosts` list of string
  - `payload` any
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `watchers.items`

Items watchers have filed, newest first: for one watcher (name), one project (project slug), or all.

- Input:
  - `limit` integer
  - `name` string
  - `project` string
- Callers: any caller

### `watchers.list`

List watchers, drafts and turned-on ones, with state (draft, on, paused, changed, invalid), schedule, next and last run, and items filed.

- Input:
  - `project` string: only this project's watchers
- Callers: any caller

### `watchers.logs`

A watcher's recent runs, newest first: when, why (schedule, retry, create, hook, event, test), items seen and filed, error and log lines.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `limit` integer
- Callers: any caller

### `watchers.pause`

Stop a watcher running until it is resumed. The pause says who stopped it.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `watchers.preset`

Write a watcher for a common source (kind: mail, calendar, repo, slack, feed, connector, pr) from a few fields, left off with its card.

- Input:
  - `kind` string, required: mail, calendar, repo, slack, feed, connector or pr. mail files short quoted notes for important mail a Gmail push announces; calendar files a note per new or changed matching event; repo, slack and feed watch new matches; connector polls a declared connector, read only; pr posts others' new comments on a session's pull requests into it as quoted data
  - `project` string, required: project slug the watcher belongs to
  - `calendar` string: calendar: calendar id, default primary
  - `channel` string: slack: channel id
  - `connection` string: mail: connection to use, default gmail; connector: id of a Connection made by the person
  - `connector` string: connector: declared connector id (gmail, google-calendar, stripe)
  - `credential` string: vault credential: Google api-credential (mail, calendar), GitHub (repo, optional if public), Slack, or the connector's
  - `dailyUsd` number
  - `days` integer: calendar: days ahead to look, default 14
  - `google` string: connector gmail or google-calendar: name of a connected Google account, instead of credential
  - `instruction` string: mail: what counts as important; optional
  - `label` string
  - `lookback_days` integer: connector: days back to read; optional
  - `match` list of string: words to look for; optional for calendar
  - `maxPerDay` integer: pr: comments posted per day, default 5
  - `only` string: repo: issues, pulls or both
  - `poll` string: connector: one of its polls
  - `repo` string: repo: owner/name
  - `session` string: pr: session id
  - `url` string: feed: feed URL
  - `vars` object: connector: what the poll needs, such as mailbox or calendar
  - `when` string: schedule; defaults: hourly (calendar, feed), every 30 minutes (repo), every 15 minutes (slack), every 10 minutes (pr)
- Callers: any caller

### `watchers.preset.target`

For the gate: the key watchers.preset acts on, watchers.preset:<project>/<kind>.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `watchers.resume`

Resume a paused watcher, clearing its failure count. The person's (or teammates' for a duty): an agent cannot undo a pause the person made.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `hash` string
- Callers: any caller

### `watchers.run`

Run a turned-on watcher now and return what happened.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `watchers.shown`

The watcher cards shown in a thread, with the hash each card carried when it was shown: { project, kinds, watchers: [{ name, hash, title, state, project, at }] }. Answered from a record made when watchers.card or watchers.preset served the card, never from the folder now. The sessions module's call.

- Input:
  - `thread` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `watchers.test`

Dry-run a watcher folder once, filing nothing. Returns the items it would emit and its logs, or what to fix. Required before watchers.create.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `event` object: a real event's payload to run an event watcher on; must match its where (hook.received: { route, id } from hooks.list)
  - `since` any: where to start reading; default null
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

## wink

### `wink.access`

What you have added with a Wink: your devices (a phone, a computer, a server, a storage device, each with its kind, who it belongs to and what it offers) and the grants given to people, as cards. Devices belong to you, not to a space. Answers { devices, grants }.

- Input:
  - `status` "active" or "revoked"
- Callers: any caller
- Registered only on the box.

### `wink.approve`

Approve a person who redeemed a sensitive invitation, after reading their fingerprint words back (the card shows them). Answers { grant }.

- Input:
  - `offer` string, required
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.
- Registered only on the box.

### `wink.cancel`

Close an offer that has not been used: its code or invitation stops working. Answers { cancelled }.

- Input:
  - `offer` string, required
- Callers: any caller
- Registered only on the box.

### `wink.card`

The card for an offer or a grant: four lines and two buttons, in the words of team/0.3/wink-copy.md. Answers { card }.

- Input:
  - `grant` string
  - `offer` string
- Callers: any caller
- Registered only on the box.

### `wink.code.ack`

Type back the code the new device is showing. One try per code: the right one adds the device and uses the code up, a wrong one closes the code and a new one is showing. Answers { ok }.

- Input:
  - `offer` string, required
  - `typed` string, required
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.
- Registered only on the box.

### `wink.code.carry`

For the spaces module: show a short typed code that carries an invitation's link to the person who types it (and whose ack is typed back with wink.code.ack). Answers { code, offer, expires } or { code: null }. One typed code shows at a time: this replaces the one showing.

- Input:
  - `link` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)
- Registered only on the box.

### `wink.code.open`

Show a short typed Wink code for a new computer or server (two-sided: the new device then shows a code to type back here, wink.code.ack). On in a release build too: the code lives 10 minutes, three wrong tries close it, and it is used once; it is refused only when the kill switch is set (VYRE_WINK_TYPED_CODE=0 or the config wink.typedCode: false), then scan the QR or paste the long code instead. Answers { offer, code, expires }. The code is a secret: it is returned here and never put on the event bus.

- Input:
  - `flow` one of "W1", "W2", "W3"
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.
- Registered only on the box.

### `wink.code.redeem`

Use a typed Wink code (WINK-NNPP-PPPP). Answers { pairing, ack, expires }: show `ack` ('type this on your other device'); the person types it there. Then wink.pair.status says `done`. For an invitation (`for` omitted or "invite") it carries `invite: { link }`, which goes to spaces.invites.accept like a pasted link. `for` phone, server or storage pairs this app with that device (target as in wink.pair.server). One try per code: a wrong code closes it. Codes last 10 minutes.

- Input:
  - `code` string, required
  - `for` one of "invite", "phone", "server", "storage"
  - `name` string
  - `target` object
    - `id` string
    - `kind` "identity" or "space"
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.
- Registered only on the box.

### `wink.code.status`

The code that is showing now, if any: { offer, code, expires, state }. The screen that opened it asks again after a replacement (a closed code is replaced with no tap).

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller
- Registered only on the box.

### `wink.decline`

Say no to a person who redeemed a sensitive invitation. Nothing is added. Answers { declined }.

- Input:
  - `offer` string, required
- Callers: any caller
- Registered only on the box.

### `wink.device.call`

Tell a connected computer something down the connection it holds (a drop is waiting). Only wink.drop.offer.

- Input:
  - `device` string, required
  - `tool` string, required
  - `input` object
- Callers: any caller
- Registered only on the box.

### `wink.device.enclave-live`

For the presence module at a paired device's sign-in: does the enclave key this device was paired with still stand on its identity's list in the directory? { ok }. Cached for 10 minutes. When the entry is gone or revoked the device is marked as needing to be paired again.

- Input:
  - `device` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)
- Registered only on the box.

### `wink.device.key`

Register the key an owner's device signs instructions with (SPKI, base64url: Ed25519 or P-256), so a headless box can take a signed instruction from it (wink.relay.apply). Needs the owner's presence. Answers { device }.

- Input:
  - `device` string, required
  - `key` string, required
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.
- Registered only on the box.

### `wink.device.paired`

For the spaces module: is this device (of any kind) one of this identity's, still paired? Answers { paired, kind? }. Modules only, read only; it names no one else's devices.

- Input:
  - `device` string, required
  - `identity` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)
- Registered only on the box.

### `wink.device.record`

What this module recorded when the owner confirmed a device: { id, kind, owner, confirmed, confirmedBy, confirmKeyId, key, hardware }, for the presence module to decide on a paired session. Only the presence module asks; null for a device the owner never confirmed.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)
- Registered only on the box.

### `wink.gate.port`

The loopback port of this box's public gate: { port } or { port: null } while there is none. Internal: the relay module's tunnel end connects each public stream to it.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)
- Registered only on the box.

### `wink.home-move.close`

Closes a move opened with wink.home-move.open: the door shuts at once. Modules only.

- Input:
  - `move_id` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)
- Registered only on the box.

### `wink.home-move.open`

The source home's spaces module opens a move for a Space: another home may then ask this home for the move's pull (spaces.moves.pull) until `expires` (a time in ms) or wink.home-move.close. Modules only.

- Input:
  - `expires` number, required
  - `move_id` string, required
  - `space` string, required
  - `to` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)
- Registered only on the box.

### `wink.home.call`

The target home's spaces module sends one request to another home through the relay and answers its data or its error code (not_found, denied, bad_input, rate_limited, plan_changed, too_large, unavailable). Only spaces.moves.pull, one request at a time. `route` and `box` are the source Space's directory record (the home's relay route and its box key); `relay` defaults to this home's own relay. Modules only.

- Input:
  - `box` string, required
  - `input` object, required
  - `route` string, required
  - `tool` string, required
  - `fresh` boolean
  - `relay` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)
- Registered only on the box.

### `wink.home.id`

The id of the server this computer is paired to, or null.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller
- Registered only on the box.

### `wink.invite`

Invite a person into this space: a Wink with the offer sealed into it (role and projects). Answers { offer, ticket, expiresAt }: show the ticket as a ring or a link. The invited person's own device redeems it and a card asks them to join; a sensitive role (admin) waits for your approval (wink.approve).

- Input:
  - `days` number
  - `name` string
  - `projects` list of string
  - `role` one of "member", "contributor", "guest", "admin"
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.
- Registered only on the box.

### `wink.network.join`

Internal, for the network module, after the owner's check and presence: bring up this machine's link to a space it has been given a way into.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller
- Registered only on the box.

### `wink.network.leave`

Internal, for the network module, after the owner's check and presence: take this machine's link to a space down.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller
- Registered only on the box.

### `wink.network.status`

Internal, for the network module: how the network looks from this machine (signed in, per space the link, direct or relayed, the relay, the server's door, storage, the clock). Read only.

- Input:
  - `ping` boolean
- Callers: any caller
- Registered only on the box.

### `wink.network.whois`
